- **M-80** Two-Mac share flow (Portty 04-06, deferred UAT). Both Macs run the new build and are signed in to the same Tailscale account.
  1. On the office Mac, turn Share on for a session running a shell, then run `seq 1 20000` in it first, so its history is larger than 32 KiB (04-10).
  2. Within 30 s the home Mac lists it under Other Macs; one click opens a tab at once (no prompt) with full control, showing that history ending in 20000.
  3. A browser on another tailnet device opens the link with full control.
  4. From Warp on the home Mac, `portty attach office-air:<session>` shows the history ending in 20000 and the status bar; `portty attach office-air` attaches when one session is shared and lists them when there are several.
  5. On the office Mac, turn Share off: the home GUI tab shows `office-air stopped sharing this session.` and its row disappears, the browser shows the same banner (the host named by its tailnet short name, the same name the GUI and CLI show, never the `.local` OS name), and the CLI prints `Portty: office-air stopped sharing <session>.` and exits 0.
  6. A Mac on a different Tailscale account shows no group for the office Mac, and `portty attach` there prints the didn't-allow-access line.
  7. Also run M-77 on both Macs.
  - _Why not automatable:_ two Macs and a live tailnet; tests use a fake local client.
  - _Source:_ 04-UI-SPEC.md UI Considerations backstop; 04-06-PLAN.md.

- **M-83** CLI attach beside a GUI tab and a browser (Portty 04-07/04-08, UAT gap 1). Both Macs run the new build and are signed in to the same Tailscale account.
  1. On the office Mac, share a shell session that has run `seq 1 20000` first (history over 32 KiB, 04-10). On the home Mac, open it from the sidebar and open its link in a browser. Type in the office window: the text shows on all three.
  2. On the home Mac run `tailscale ping <office>` and note whether it answers via DERP or directly.
  3. From Warp on the home Mac run `portty attach <office>:"<session>"`. Watch the GUI tab and the browser for 15 s, type a command in the CLI, then detach with `Ctrl-\`. Repeat attach and detach three times.
     - Expected: the GUI tab and the browser never blank or freeze, every command shows on every viewer, and the bar's viewer count is 4 (office window, GUI tab, browser, CLI).
     - On an idle session the bar never shows `[reconnecting]`; it appears only while the connection is really down (UAT gap 1, fixed in 05-06).
  4. If a viewer blanks or freezes, note the time. On both Macs run `grep viewer-diag "$HOME/Library/Application Support/portty/daemon.log"` (and `daemon.log.1` if the daemon restarted). Keep the lines from 30 s before to 30 s after, plus the `tailscale ping` result. Read them for:
     - a `grid` line with small dimensions (host-side grid push, H-grid);
     - `slow` lines (a stalled viewer was kicked);
     - a CLI `first input` within a second of its `join`. 04-07 fixed H-replies (the replay no longer carries terminal queries), so this is a regression check: it should not appear;
     - the home Mac's `proxy open` dial time and its `proxy end` lines (which side ended first, close code). A slow dial points at a cold or DERP-relayed path (H-net).
     - a home-Mac `proxy end` line with first=peer, code=-1 and an err containing `message too big` right after `proxy open`. That was the 32 KiB read limit fixed in 04-10, so it should not appear.
  - _Why not automatable:_ two Macs, a real tailnet path (direct or DERP), Warp's terminal replies and the native GUI. internal/daemon/attach_viewers_test.go covers the code paths with fakes.
  - _Source:_ 04-UAT.md gap 1; 04-07-PLAN.md and 04-08-PLAN.md.


### Category AT — Overnight Survival (SHAR-07, VERF-01)

The user requires Portty to be rock solid: a session must survive network glitches, network and Wi-Fi access-point switches, and sleep/wake with no manual reattach. M-84 and M-85 are **deferred-capable**: at Phase 5 verification the user decides whether M-84 (the real overnight run, VERF-01 as written) or M-85 (the accelerated sleep/wake run) gates VERF-01, or defers both. Claude runs M-86 and M-87 in the Tart VMs (Safari through dev-browser); only the two-Mac items need the user. Two Tart VMs are on the tailnet for two-machine runs: `portty-test` (office role, tailnet name `portty-vm-office-2`, 100.x.x.N) and `portty-test-2` (home role, `portty-vm-home-3`, 100.x.x.N).

Caveats a user can trip over (all by design or known, none a failure by itself):
- zsh `clear` sends `ESC[3J`, which empties the history a reattach replays.
- History lives in memory only (last 10,000 lines), so a restart of the host Mac's daemon loses it, as before Phase 5.
- A browser cannot tell a refused probe from an unreachable or sleeping host, so it never ends a session on a network error and keeps retrying until the page is closed. This covers the host Mac's daemon dying while the page is open (05-VM-MATRIX.md B5) and the host turning Share off while a browser is asleep with no other session shared (the share port is closed; 05-05 known limit). After about 2 minutes of failed probes (refused or the 10 s timeout) the banner changes from `Connection lost — reconnecting…` to `Can't reach <host>. The session may have ended or the Mac may be asleep. Still retrying…`. The first answer from the share server clears it. A 401/403 still shows `<host> stopped sharing this session.`, and a 404 shows the session-ended text. The GUI tab and the CLI are unchanged: the local daemon maps a refused dial to the final stop.
- Both Macs must run the same build. A new client against an older host gets full replays instead of deltas, and its watchdog stays armed.
- The office Mac must be kept awake by hand (a foreground `caffeinate`). Portty has no keep-awake feature (CONV-01, v2) and these checks never change power or sleep settings.
- After the viewing Mac wakes or changes network, the GUI tab, the browser view and the CLI probe an open socket or redial at once instead of waiting out the watchdog (05-10 CLI, 05-13 GUI tab and browser: the pure-Go detector `internal/wakewatch` sees a wall-clock tick gap or a changed interface address list; in a browser a late watchdog tick counts as a wake). Expect recovery in about 10 s (05-VM-MATRIX.md R4: 9.4 and 11.8 s after the viewing VM resumed). With no wake or network change on the viewing side (the host Mac slept, or the link went silent) the 45 s watchdog (40 s of silence plus a 5 s probe) notices the dead socket, and the GUI tab's daemon proxy always relies on it; after a host sleep the backoff can add more (R5: 32 s and 46 s after the host resumed).
- If the viewing Mac's daemon dies, the GUI's 5 s tray poller or a running `portty attach` starts it again (05-16). There is no launchd agent, so with neither running nothing restarts it until the next launch.

- **M-84** Overnight two-Mac test (Portty 05-07; VERF-01 as written; deferred-capable). Both Macs run the same build and are signed in to the same Tailscale account.
  1. On the office Mac open a terminal (not Portty) and run `caffeinate -dims`. Leave it in the foreground all night and stop it with Ctrl-C in the morning. Change no system setting.
  2. On the office Mac start Claude Code in a Portty session and turn Share on. On the home Mac open the session from the sidebar, and in Warp run `portty attach <office>:<session>`.
  3. Leave both overnight. The home Mac may sleep.
  4. Next morning, without reattaching:
     - typing reaches Claude Code from the GUI tab and from the CLI;
     - the previous day's scrollback is there in both, with nothing duplicated;
     - the same session opens from a browser on another tailnet device and works.
  5. Long-session reattach (05-08/05-12): after a long Claude Code session (hours of output, at least a few hundred KiB), close the home GUI tab and the CLI, then reattach from the home GUI sidebar and with `portty attach`. Compare each with the office window: the same rows, no staircase or jumbled lines, and typing reaches Claude Code.
  6. On both Macs run `grep viewer-diag "$HOME/Library/Application Support/portty/daemon.log"` (and `daemon.log.1` if a daemon restarted). Read the `replay ... mode=` lines (reconnects should be `mode=delta` unless the 256 KiB ring wrapped) and any `heartbeat timeout` / `proxy heartbeat timeout` lines (a dead socket was dropped).
  - _Why not automatable:_ two real Macs, a real overnight idle gap with sleep, the real tailnet path and the native GUI. The accelerated stand-ins are in internal/daemon/attach_viewers_test.go and internal/relay/history_test.go (Section 4 VERF-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 05-RESEARCH.md Testing Strategy; 05-01..05-06 plans; 05-CONTEXT.md (VERF-01 gate deferred).

- **M-85** Two-Mac short sleep/wake and network changes (Portty 05-07; deferred-capable accelerated alternative to M-84). Same setup as M-84 steps 1-2, plus the session open in a browser on the home Mac.
  1. Close the home Mac's lid for 10+ minutes, then open it. All three viewers reconnect on their own: the GUI tab and the browser may show `Connection lost — reconnecting…` briefly, and the CLI bar may show `[reconnecting]` briefly.
  2. Turn the home Mac's Wi-Fi off for 20 s and back on. Then switch it to another Wi-Fi network or access point (or a phone hotspot) and back.
  3. Expected after each step: history is intact with nothing duplicated, and typing into Claude Code works from all three, with no manual reattach.
     - While the home daemon cannot reach the office Mac, the CLI bar may flip from `[reconnecting]` to connected for a moment and back (the proxy accepts the loopback socket, then closes it with 1013 "remote unreachable"; 05-06 known behavior).
  4. After a long Claude Code session, reattach from the home GUI and the CLI and compare with the office window as in M-84 step 5: same rows, no staircase, typing works.
  5. Read the daemon.log `viewer-diag` lines as in M-84 step 6.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real Mac's sleep and Wi-Fi, the GUI tab inside WKWebView and the real tailnet path. The VMs cannot sleep the way a laptop lid does.
  - _Source:_ 05-RESEARCH.md Testing Strategy; 05-04, 05-05 and 05-06 plans.

- **M-86** VM browser freeze (Portty 05-07; Claude runs it).
  1. Build with `wails build -platform darwin/arm64 -tags "wailsassets vmpreview"`, copy Portty.app into the Tart VM `portty-test`, open a session, turn Share on and open the `127.0.0.1:7444` link in Safari. Type `echo hi`: it runs.
  2. Find the daemon (`pgrep -fl portty`) and `kill -STOP` it for 60 s. The page shows `Connection lost — reconnecting…` (watchdog: about 40 s of silence plus a 5 s probe) and ignores typing.
  3. `kill -CONT` it. The page reconnects, the banner clears, nothing is duplicated and typing works.
  4. Two-VM variant: with Share on in `portty-test`, open the session in `portty-test-2` from the sidebar, with `portty attach portty-vm-office-2:<session>` and in Safari on the tailnet link. Repeat steps 2-3 against the office daemon, then `kill -STOP` / `-CONT` the home daemon in `portty-test-2`. All three home viewers recover with nothing duplicated.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real browser's WebSocket and timers against a frozen daemon; jsdom fakes the socket. The rules are covered by relayClient.test.ts and TerminalPanel.reconnect.test.tsx.
  - _Source:_ 05-RESEARCH.md Testing Strategy (VM list); 05-04-PLAN.md.

- **M-87** VM suspend / clock jump (Portty 05-07; Claude runs it).
  1. With the M-86 setup (one VM or both), run `tart suspend portty-test` for several minutes, then resume it.
  2. Expected: the session continues with no reconnect storm (only a few `join` lines in daemon.log) and no duplicated output; typing works. In the two-VM setup, also suspend and resume `portty-test-2` (the home role) and check its GUI tab, CLI and browser recover.
  3. If `tart suspend` does not work for this guest, run the M-86 `kill -STOP` variant instead and note it.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a guest suspend jumps the wall clock under a live WebSocket; the CLI watchdog measures wall-clock silence on purpose (05-06), which a unit test cannot reproduce faithfully.
  - _Source:_ 05-RESEARCH.md Testing Strategy (VM list) and Assumption A7; 05-06-PLAN.md.

- **M-88** Duplicate remote tab and close (Portty 05-15; Claude runs it in the two Tart VMs).
  1. Real build (`wails build -platform darwin/arm64 -tags wailsassets`) in both VMs. In `portty-test` (office) open a session and turn Share on.
  2. In `portty-test-2` (home) double-click the session's row under "Other Macs" while its connect is still in flight.
  3. Expected: exactly one tab and one live terminal; the office daemon.log shows one web viewer join for it.
  4. Close the tab with Cmd+W. Expected: the sidebar, the tab bar and the "No Open Sessions" empty state stay, the app does not restart, and no ErrorBoundary fallback shows. One click on the row reopens one tab; close and double-click again gives one tab again.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real double click racing a real peer connect through the native WKWebView window; App.open-remote.test.tsx, tabs.test.ts and ErrorBoundary.test.tsx cover the rules in jsdom.
  - _Source:_ 05-09 (todo duplicate-remote-tab-blank-window); last run V-09 PASS in .planning/phases/05-overnight-survival/05-VM-MATRIX.md (2026-11-18).

- **M-89** Guest reattach rendering and scrollbar at the pane edge (Portty 05-15; Claude runs it in the two Tart VMs).
  1. Same setup as M-88. In the office session print more than 300 KiB of Claude-Code-like output (truecolor tool lines, soft-wrapping paragraphs, CJK and emoji, box-drawn input frames, spinner redraws), ending at a prompt.
  2. Open the session in the home VM from the sidebar. Expected: every visible row matches the office window (daemon.log shows `replay ... mode=full`); type a command and it runs in the office shell; both windows show the same rows afterwards.
  3. Close the tab and reopen it: the rows match again. Widen the office window with the home tab open and stream more output: the rows still match.
  4. Scrollbar: resize the home window so the guest is height-limited, width-limited and then narrower than the host grid. Expected: the scrollbar sits at the right edge of the pane in the first two cases, and the pane scrolls sideways in the third.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real xterm.js rendering in WKWebView at a real scale, compared with a real office window; replayFidelity.test.ts and TerminalPanel.scale.test.tsx cover the grid and the geometry in jsdom. Reading rows in a VM needs an evidence build with `screenReaderMode: true` (never committed) because WebKit cannot be screen-captured there.
  - _Source:_ 05-08 and 05-12 (todos jumbled-screen-on-reattach and guest-scrollbar-at-pane-edge); last run V-12 PASS in .planning/phases/05-overnight-survival/05-VM-MATRIX.md (2026-11-18).

- **M-90** VM resilience matrix R1-R9 (Portty 05-15; Claude runs it in the two Tart VMs).
  1. Real build with real Tailscale in both VMs (start each with `tart run --suspendable` so R4 and R5 can use `tart suspend`). In the office VM run a shared session with a 2 s ticker; in the home VM attach a GUI tab and `portty attach`.
  2. Run each row and check, for each viewer: it recovers with no manual reattach, no tick is missing or repeated, and a typed `echo <ROW>-OK` lands once in the office shell. Faults go through a pf anchor inside the VM; nothing runs on the host Mac.
     - R1 brief drop (15 s silent block); R2 half-open (70 s silent block); R3 interface down/up and address changes; R4 viewing VM suspend; R5 host VM suspend; R6 viewing daemon `kill -9` (GUI alone, CLI alone, both); R7 host daemon `kill -9` (must end as "stopped sharing", not reconnect); R8 more than 256 KiB of output during a 76 s block; R9 transient Tailscale faults must never read as "stopped sharing".
  3. Record each row in 05-VM-MATRIX.md (or the next phase's evidence file) as PASS or FAIL, with reconnect seconds and replay mode. No share links or cap values.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-18, build `39b0153e` (R6 re-run on `1e0c33c3`): 9 PASS, 0 FAIL. R1 PASS (no reconnect, TCP carried the gap); R2 PASS (1.6 / 2.6 s after the block lifted, delta); R3 PASS (1.5 / 2.0 s after the interface came up, delta; an address change with a working route caused no reconnect); R4 PASS (9.4 / 11.8 s after resume, delta); R5 PASS (32 s GUI / 46 s CLI after the host resumed, delta); R6 PASS (1.4 to 5 s, delta, after the 05-16 respawn fix); R7 PASS (final "stopped sharing" in 1 to 3 s, no reconnect); R8 PASS (14 / 18 s, full replay, every line once); R9 PASS (never "stopped sharing", delta each time). R3 covered an interface flap and address changes only: a real Wi-Fi access-point switch is covered only by M-85. Browser views were not in this run (M-86 covers them).
  - _Why not automatable:_ real tailnet paths (DERP-relayed between the VMs), real suspend and real daemon kills across two machines; the unit stand-ins are the Section 4 SHAR-07 and VERF-01 rows.
  - _Source:_ 05-14 and 05-16 (todo network-resilience-matrix); evidence in .planning/phases/05-overnight-survival/05-VM-MATRIX.md.

- **M-91** UI polish: accent icons, remote tab label, per-Mac dot, amber reconnecting dot (quick ui-polish-four-upgrades; Claude runs it in the two Tart VMs).
  1. Real build in both VMs. In the office VM create a session and Share it; in the home VM create a local session and open both the local and the office session from the sidebar.
  2. Expected, in Light and in Dark (`~/ptest/appear light|dark`): sidebar row icons in the accent colour (white on the selected row); the remote tab reads `shell 1 · <host>` with the host dimmed, a globe before it and the office Mac's colour dot after it, both aria-hidden; the same dot ends the office session's sidebar row; a narrow window ellipsizes the label while the globe and dot keep their size.
  3. In the home VM block the office tailnet IP with the pf anchor (`~/ptest/pf.sh block <office-ip> drop`). Expected: once the tab shows `Connection lost — reconnecting…`, the office host row's dot is amber with the accessible name "Reconnecting", and the host row stays listed (with the can't-reach line) even though discovery drops the blocked Mac. `pf.sh clear`: the dot returns to green "Reachable" and the session row comes back.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-18, commit `1c32da40` (evidence build with an uncommitted probe mirroring computed styles into the AX tree, then the clean build redeployed). Light: icons rgb(0,99,204), office dot rgb(255,45,85), tab host rgb(99,99,102). Dark: icons rgb(77,163,255), dot rgb(255,55,95), host rgb(161,161,166). Amber rgb(255,159,10) 43 s after the block in both schemes; green 20 s after the clear. The first run found the vanishing host row, fixed in `d7e76093`.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real reconnect timing and the daemon's peer discovery under a real link drop, read from WKWebView's computed styles. The rules are covered by sidebarPeers.test.ts, Sidebar.test.tsx, TabBar.test.tsx, TerminalPanel.reconnect.test.tsx and App.open-remote.test.tsx.
  - _Source:_ .planning/todos/completed/2026-11-18-ui-polish-four-upgrades.md; sketch .planning/sketches/001-app-polish/.

### Category AU — Release Updates (UPD-01..UPD-04)

- **M-92** VM update checks U-01..U-06 (Portty 06-04; Claude runs it in the two Tart VMs).
  1. Build on the host: `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11 wails build -platform darwin/arm64 -tags "wailsassets vmpreview" -ldflags "-X main.Version=v0.9.0"`, then `git checkout -- frontend/src/wailsjs`. The `vmpreview` tag (updates_vmpreview.go) points the checks at `http://127.0.0.1:8788` with a 15 s tick and a 2 min cadence; never use it in a release build. Build cmd/fakegh with `GOOS=darwin GOARCH=arm64 go build ./cmd/fakegh`.
  2. Inputs: a notarized DMG from Team S2K7P43927 for the reveal (2026-11-18 used GIFsmith v1.0.1's `Gifsmith-1.0.1-arm64.dmg`, sha256 4119ccd1…faa1, served under Portty's asset name). Since the WR-01 fix (06-REVIEW-FIX) Verify also requires the signing identifier to be the asset name, so that DMG is now refused at the identifier step (U-04-style signature text) and the U-05 reveal needs a notarized Portty release DMG served under its own asset name, which exists only from the first release (M-93); `unsigned.dmg` from `hdiutil create -format UDZO` of a folder with one text file; `adhoc.dmg`, a copy after `codesign -s -`. Copy Portty.app, fakegh and the DMGs to each VM's ~/ptest. Back up settings.json first.
  3. Clean state before each scenario: kill Portty, remove `~/Library/Application Support/portty/update_notified` and `~/Library/Caches/app.portty.Portty/updates`. Run `~/ptest/fakegh -scenario <newer|current|draft|prerelease|noasset|baddigest|404|ratelimit> -version <v> -dmg <file>` and launch with `open --stdout ~/ptest/gui.log --stderr ~/ptest/gui.log ~/ptest/Portty.app`. Read the UI with `~/ptest/axdump`, press Help → Check for Updates… with `axmenu`, click with `click`.
     - U-01 (office): newer 9.9.9. About 10 s after launch: one fakegh request, the banner "Portty 9.9.9 is available." with Download, one `notification: dispatching` and one `update: notified 9.9.9` line, the tray menu has "Update Available…".
     - U-02 (office): current 0.9.0 at launch is quiet; switch to newer 9.9.9: the banner and one notification at the next periodic check; the next request is `inm=yes 304` with no second notification; switch to 9.9.10: banner and one notification for it.
     - U-03 (office): manual check gives "Portty is up to date." (current), "Version 9.9.10 is available." with Download and Later (Later downloads nothing), "Couldn't check for updates." with the network text (fakegh stopped) and with the rate-limit text (ratelimit); no notified line from manual checks.
     - U-04 (office): Download in the banner with unsigned.dmg, adhoc.dmg and baddigest: the signature or checksum text with Try Again, the updates folder empty, no Finder window on the file.
     - U-05 (office): Download in the banner with the notarized DMG, then (file removed) from the Check for Updates… alert: "Verifying…", then "verified and shown in Finder"; Finder's front window is the updates folder with the DMG selected; `stat -f %Lp` 600; `xattr -p com.apple.quarantine` contains `;Portty;`.
     - U-06 (home): 404, draft, prerelease, noasset, ratelimit and fakegh stopped: at least two automatic attempts, no banner, notification or alert; a manual check says "Portty is up to date." for 404, draft and prerelease. noasset (a newer release without its DMG) is an incomplete check since the WR-04 fix (06-REVIEW-FIX): automatic checks stay quiet and retry on the next tick, and a manual check says "Couldn't check for updates." / "Version 9.9.9 isn't ready to download yet. Try again later."
  4. Record each row as PASS or FAIL with evidence. Afterwards redeploy a clean non-vmpreview build, restore settings.json and leave nothing listening on 8788.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-18, build 5cfa6b4d plus the 06-04 seam (f562372e): U-01..U-06 all PASS; evidence in .planning/phases/06-release-updates/06-VM-CHECKS.md. The ad-hoc wails build may not post notifications (`Notifications are not allowed for this application`), so delivery was checked only as one dispatch per version; M-93 checks delivery.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the native NSAlert, the notification, Finder's reveal and the real codesign/spctl need a GUI session on real macOS. The logic is covered by the Section 4 UPD rows.
  - _Source:_ 06-CONTEXT.md specifics (fake GitHub in a Tart VM through a build seam; reject an unsigned DMG; reveal in Finder); 06-04-PLAN.md.
  - _Note (Phase 18):_ from Phase 18 the banner button is Update and installs the new version itself; U-05's reveal applies only to install failures (M-168). U-01 and U-03's Download wording is now Update.

- **M-93** First tagged release, end to end (Portty 06-04; on the real Macs at the first `v*` tag).
  - _Preconditions:_ scottkw/portty is public, and the release workflow's Actions secrets and `release` environment exist.
  1. The release job's "Verify DMG like the in-app updater" step passed.
  2. On the host, with the downloaded release DMG: `UPDATE_REAL_DMG=<dmg> GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11 go test -run TestRealDMG ./internal/updater` and `UPDATE_RELEASE_JSON=<saved releases/latest JSON> UPDATE_DMG_PATH=<dmg> GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11 go test -run TestReleaseJSON ./internal/updater` pass (not skipped).
  3. On the home Mac, an installed older release shows the update banner about 10 s after launch and a delivered notification attributed to Portty; Help → Check for Updates… offers the new version; from Phase 18, Update installs the new version and relaunches it (the DMG is revealed in Finder only when the install fails, M-168). An older release without self-install still reveals the DMG, which opens and installs.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs the real signed and notarized release on GitHub and a real installed older version.
  - _Source:_ 06-04-PLAN.md; M-41 for notification delivery on a signed build.

### Category AV — Hardening (HARD-01, HARD-02, HARD-04, HARD-05)

Rig for all four (Portty 07-05; Claude runs them in the two Tart VMs): build on the host with `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11 wails build -platform darwin/arm64 -tags wailsassets` (the real app, never vmpreview), then `git checkout -- frontend/src/wailsjs`. Deploy `build/bin/Portty.app` to `~/ptest/` in the office VM (portty-test) and the home VM (portty-test-2), launch with `open --stderr /tmp/<name>.err ~/ptest/Portty.app`, read the GUI with `~/ptest/axdump` and click and type with the click helper. Daemon logs: `~/Library/Application Support/portty/daemon.log`.

- **M-94** Large paste over the tailnet (HARD-02).
  1. Generate exactly 100,000 base64 bytes with no newline in the home VM: `head -c 75000 /dev/urandom | base64 | tr -d '\n' | head -c 100000`; record its `shasum -a 256`.
  2. GUI peer tab: open an office session from Other Macs in the home GUI, run `stty raw -echo; head -c 100000 > /tmp/<file>; stty sane` in it, `pbcopy` the payload and paste it with Cmd+V. The office file is 100,000 bytes with the payload's sha256, and the home daemon.log shows no socket close for that viewer.
  3. `portty attach`: the same receiver typed through the expect driver (100x30 pty, FIFO input); write the payload into the FIFO in one write; compare size and sha256.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-18, build fb72d7b9: H-01 (GUI peer tab) and H-02 (`portty attach`) PASS, both office files 100,000 bytes with the payload sha256, no 1009 close; evidence in .planning/phases/07-hardening/07-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a native Cmd+V paste into the GUI and a real tailnet between two Macs need a GUI session on real macOS. The split is covered by the Section 4 HARD-02 rows.
  - _Source:_ 07-CONTEXT.md specifics (VM spot check); 07-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-95** Peer tab switch rejoins with a delta replay (HARD-05).
  1. Two shared office sessions, one running a 2 s TICK ticker. In the home GUI open the ticker tab, then the other tab, wait 20 s, select the ticker tab again; three times.
  2. Each rejoin logs `viewer-diag: replay ... mode=delta ... resume=true` in the office daemon.log, and the tab's ticks are consecutive with none missing or repeated across each switch.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-18, build fb72d7b9: H-03 PASS, three returns logged `mode=delta ... resume=true`, ticks 1..291 consecutive with no repeat; evidence in .planning/phases/07-hardening/07-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ native tab clicks in the GUI against a real remote daemon. The logic is covered by the Section 4 HARD-05 rows.
  - _Source:_ 07-CONTEXT.md; 07-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-96** GUI launch with a healthy daemon makes no respawn attempt (HARD-04).
  1. With the home daemon healthy for more than 60 s, kill only the GUI app process (it ignores SIGTERM, so `kill -9` its pid; never the `Portty daemon` process), relaunch with `open --stderr /tmp/h4-N.err`, wait 30 s; three launches.
  2. No `respawn attempt` line in any capture, and the same daemon pid throughout. If a `not answering right after launch` line appears, record its error text (the HARD-04 root-cause evidence).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 7dcb66b2: V-06 PASS, three GUI relaunches with daemon 1625 healthy, 0 capture lines, no respawn line, the same daemon pid; evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs a real GUI launch with the app's own daemon spawn path on macOS. The gate is covered by the Section 4 HARD-04 row.
  - _Source:_ 07-03-SUMMARY.md (HARD-04 root cause); 07-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-97** Alt-screen soak memory on a real daemon (HARD-01, DEBT-01).
  1. Quit the office GUI and daemon; start `GODEBUG=gctrace=1 nohup ~/ptest/Portty.app/Contents/MacOS/Portty daemon > /tmp/gctrace.log 2>&1 &`; relaunch the GUI.
  2. Attach with the expect driver at the width under test (100x30, then 220x50) and record C = `tput cols`. Enter the alt screen (`printf '\e[?1049h'`), print 30,000 lines of exactly C non-blank characters, leave (`printf '\e[?1049l'`).
  3. Read the live heap (the last number of each `gc N ...: A->B->C MB` line) at baseline, in the alt screen and after at least two GCs past the exit. PASS: at C = 100 and at C = 220 the in-alt peak minus baseline is at most a fixed 32 MiB (the alt screen keeps no scrollback since 08-01), and the post-exit live heap is within 20 MB of baseline after each run. Record RSS alongside. Restore the normal GUI-spawned daemon afterwards.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 7dcb66b2 (the code of 390f832f): V-01 PASS, in-alt peak minus baseline 5 MiB at C = 100 (counting the alt-exit GC) and 0 MiB at C = 220, post-exit +1 MB and +0 MB; evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real daemon process with a real PTY and TUI-style output over minutes; the unit-level bound is the Section 4 HARD-01 and DEBT-01 rows.
  - _Source:_ 07-01-SUMMARY.md (measured heap growth); 07-05-PLAN.md; 08-01-SUMMARY.md (the fixed bound); 08-05-PLAN.md.

### Category AW — Debt Cleanup (DEBT-02..DEBT-06)

Rig as for Category AV (Portty 08-05; Claude runs them in the two Tart VMs, WebGL off in both VMs' settings.json for the run). Office-side typing uses a local `portty attach` through the expect driver (`cli.sh` / `cli.exp`, 100x30); home GUI peer tabs are opened with `openrow.sh`, selected with `clicktab.sh`, read with `~/ptest/axdump2` and copied with `~/ptest/gcopy.sh`. Replay lines in the office daemon.log read `viewer-diag: replay session=... mode=... bytes=... resume=...`.

- **M-98** A soft-wrapped line in a rendered replay (DEBT-02).
  1. In a shared office session at 100 columns run `seq 1 60000` first, so the 256 KiB ring wraps and a join takes the rendered path (run afterwards, it would push the lines below out of the 10,000-line history), then type `echo ` and a 150-character token (the command line soft-wraps), `printf '=%.0s' $(seq 1 100); echo` and `echo AFTER-EQ`.
  2. Open a fresh home GUI peer tab; the office log shows `mode=full`. The tab's copy has the echo command on one line with the whole token, the 100-character `=` line and `AFTER-EQ` on separate lines, and the seq tail consecutive.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 7dcb66b2: V-02 PASS, rendered replay (`mode=full`, 70,902 bytes), the 182-character echo command on one line with the whole token, the `=` line and `AFTER-EQ` separate, the seq tail consecutive; evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real WebKit xterm.js tab over a real tailnet against a real daemon. The relay logic is covered by the Section 4 DEBT-02 rows.
  - _Source:_ 08-01-SUMMARY.md; 07-VM-CHECKS.md R-02 (the split it fixes); 08-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-99** A rendered replay after `reset` keeps history and cursor (DEBT-03 IN-06).
  1. In a shared office session run `seq -f 'pre %g' 1 30`, `reset`, `seq -f 'post %g' 1 60`, then force the rendered path with a host resize from the local driver (100x30 to 110x30; the office log shows `grid ... by=local`).
  2. Open a fresh home GUI peer tab. Its copy has `post 1` .. `post 60` consecutive with none missing, the cursor (the `xterm-helper-textarea` AX frame) is on the prompt row after `post 60`, and `echo CURSOR-OK-V03` from the office driver appears after that prompt.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 7dcb66b2: V-03 PASS, `post 1..60` consecutive with no duplicate after a genuine 100x30 to 110x30 resize, the cursor on the live prompt row, `echo CURSOR-OK-V03` landed after it; evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real WebKit xterm.js viewer joining a real daemon's rendered replay. The order is covered by the Section 4 DEBT-03 rows.
  - _Source:_ 08-02-SUMMARY.md; 07-VM-CHECKS.md R-03; 08-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-100** Peer-tab snapshot lifecycle (DEBT-04).
  1. Two shared office sessions as home GUI tabs, one a 2 s TICK ticker. Three times: 20 s on the other tab, then select the ticker tab. Then a quick switch: the other tab and back within about 1 s. Then close the ticker tab with its close button and reopen the same session from Other Macs.
  2. Each return logs `mode=delta ... resume=true` in the office daemon.log and the ticks are consecutive with none repeated (`seqcheck.sh TICK`); the reopen after the close logs `mode=full ... resume=false`.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 7dcb66b2: V-04 PASS, three returns and a quick switch (1.05 s) all `mode=delta ... resume=true`, ticks 1..181 consecutive with no repeat, the reopen after the close `mode=full ... resume=false`; evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ native tab clicks and a tab close in the GUI against a real remote daemon. The store logic is covered by the Section 4 DEBT-04 rows.
  - _Source:_ 08-03-SUMMARY.md; M-95; 08-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-101** Partial-paste notice on a tailnet drop (DEBT-05).
  1. Main run: in a home GUI tab on a shared office session run `stty raw -echo; cat > /tmp/<file>`, paste a large base64 payload (P bytes, 5 MB or more, no newline, sha256 recorded) with Cmd+V, and drop the tailnet path within about 1 s (an office watcher can start the drop the moment the file is non-empty). The drop is either a silent pf block of the office share port (used from Phase 10 on, because the VMs are ephemeral tailnet nodes: `block drop quick on <utun> proto tcp from any to any port <share port>` and its reverse in the anchor `com.apple/portty-drop`, enabled with `pfctl -E`, plus a nohup'd safety net that flushes and releases it; `tailscaled` stays up and the node stays online) or `tailscale down` in the office VM. Lift it once the tab shows the notice (`tailscale up` after 15 s for the second form). PASS when all hold: the tab shows a paste notice, either `Paste may have been cut short (M KB)` or `Paste interrupted: N of M KB sent`, with M = ceil(P/1024), never silence; after a drop the host still delivers every byte it had read (since Phase 10, INPT-01: the input queue outlives the socket), so the office file is an exact prefix of the payload (`cmp -n`) whose length is what the office read (that conn's `input total` bytes), and it stops growing once that queue drains (allow the drain time before the "stops growing" check; nothing is resent); the tab takes typing again. On this GUI peer path the browser hands the whole paste to the local daemon proxy over loopback, so `bufferedAmount` is 0 when the tailnet side dies and the "may have been cut short" wording is the expected one (08-VM-CHECKS.md, orchestrator disposition of V-05). The GUI's WebContent must also go idle after the paste (08-REVIEW WR-01); that is checked by M-103.
  2. Sub-run: drop first (either form), then paste a 100,000-byte payload within 3 s. Once the tab shows the reconnecting banner, `tailscale up`. Same PASS rule with M = 98: a paste notice in either wording, never silence, and the office file is an exact prefix of the payload that does not grow.
  - _Variant (optional):_ the `Paste interrupted: N of M KB sent` wording with N < M and an office file under (N+1) × 1024 bytes applies only where the browser's own socket backs up (a browser web-share viewer on a slow link); it is unit-tested in relayClient.test.ts and not required here.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build 35aaa035 (B v1.2.0-vmB): R-03 PASS, mechanism pf: a 5,000,000-byte paste cut 0.1 s after its first bytes showed `Paste may have been cut short (4883 KB)` about 21 s into the drop; the office file (65,534 bytes) was an exact prefix equal to that conn's `input total` and never grew; the tab rejoined with a delta and took typing. Evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md. Earlier: 2026-11-19, build 7dcb66b2: V-05 recorded FAIL under the old rule, which demanded the N-of-M wording; dispositioned as meeting DEBT-05 (08-VM-CHECKS.md, orchestrator disposition of V-05) and the rule above rewritten to match (08-REVIEW WR-05). Under it the run passes: drops mid-paste at 5 MB and 20 MB both showed `Paste may have been cut short (M KB)`, the office files (98,301 and 262,136 bytes) were exact prefixes that never grew, and the tab took typing again. A 20 MB paste also pinned the GUI's WebContent for over 15 minutes (the pasted text stayed in xterm's helper textarea; fixed by 08-REVIEW WR-01 and re-run as M-103, which passed on build 5e48b6f2). The sub-run was not run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a native Cmd+V paste, a real tailnet outage between two Macs and the browser's real socket buffering. The accounting is covered by the Section 4 DEBT-05 rows.
  - _Source:_ 08-03-SUMMARY.md; M-94; 08-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-102** Cold launch after a reboot (DEBT-06).
  1. Reboot the home VM with no daemon running (kill the GUI and the daemon with `kill -9` first, so the stale `daemon.sock` stays), remove Portty from loginwindow's relaunch list, and after the boot launch with `open --stderr /tmp/<name>.err ~/ptest/Portty.app`; wait 30 s. Also once with the relaunch-list entry kept, so macOS reopens Portty at login (its stderr is /dev/null: the byte count of its fd 2 is the check, 0 or the 163-byte AppKit line).
  2. No `respawn attempt` or `not answering right after launch` line, exactly one daemon (ppid = the GUI), and no `daemon: already running` in daemon.log / daemon.log.1.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 7dcb66b2: V-06 PASS, a cold `open --stderr` launch 45 s after a reboot (one daemon, only the AppKit line) and a macOS reopen at login (GUI fd 2 offset 163, no `already running`); evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real reboot and macOS's login-time relaunch. The wait is covered by the Section 4 DEBT-06 row.
  - _Source:_ 08-04-SUMMARY.md and 08-VM-CHECKS.md (L- and B-rows); 08-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-103** A large paste leaves the GUI idle (08-REVIEW WR-01, DEBT-05).
  1. In a home GUI tab on a shared office session run `stty raw -echo; head -c P > /tmp/<file>; stty sane` with P = 20000000, then Cmd+V a 20,000,000-byte base64 payload (no newline, sha256 recorded). Sample Portty's WebContent CPU about once a second (`top -l 2 -s 1`, second sample) for 2 minutes. Repeat in a home local tab with a 5,000,000-byte payload and `head -c 5000000` (P must equal the payload size, or `head` never finishes). Then type, paste a short line and paste unicode text in both tabs.
  2. PASS when all hold: WebContent CPU is back to idle (no sustained samples over 5 %) within about 30 s of the paste; the office file is complete (P bytes, sha256 and `cmp` equal to the payload); an AX read of the window returns at once and `xterm-helper-textarea` has an empty value; typing and small, bracketed and unicode pastes still arrive unchanged.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 5e48b6f2: P-01 PASS (20 MB peer tab: WebContent 6.9 % at the paste, no sample over 5 % after 30 s, file complete at 14 s with sha256 and `cmp` equal, AX dump 0.05 s with no textarea value; 5 MB local tab: file complete at 2 s, only isolated single samples over 5 % after 30 s) and P-02 PASS (typing, small, bracketed and unicode pastes in both tabs); evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ WKWebView's native Edit-menu paste and WebKit's caret-blink re-shaping cannot run in jsdom. The guard's wiring and the modelled default insertion are covered by the Section 4 DEBT-05 row for pasteGuard.test.ts.
  - _Source:_ 08-REVIEW.md WR-01 and IN-12 (iterations 1 and 2), IN-16 (iteration 3: the receiver's byte count matches each payload); 08-VM-CHECKS.md P-01, P-02.

### Category AX — Upgrade Guard (DEBT-08)

Rig: the home VM only, two ad-hoc signed builds of the same commit that differ only in `-X main.Version=` (A and B), installed in place at `~/ptest/Portty.app` with `rm -rf` and `ditto` (08-VM-CHECKS.md U-01 method). Sessions are made with A's CLI and given a ticker through the expect driver (`cli.sh`); `/sessions/<id>/tail` over the daemon socket shows them advancing.

- **M-104** An upgrade asks before it ends live sessions (DEBT-08). Since Phase 9 the question appears only when the running daemon cannot hand off (a v1.1 daemon, no `handoff` in `/health`) or the handoff failed (M-107, M-108); a successful handoff asks nothing (M-105). So A here is a v1.1 build (or a vmpreview B-fault takeover, as in M-107), and B is a v1.2 build.
  1. GUI, Not Now: A's GUI and two ticking sessions; quit A's GUI (the A daemon stays); install and launch B. The window shows "Update Portty's background service?" with "2 sessions are running. Restarting ends them.", Not Now and Restart Now. Click Not Now: the dialog closes, both tabs are open on the A daemon (same pid, `/health` still A), typing into each tab reaches its shell, and no prompt comes back during the launch (30 s).
  2. GUI, Restart Now: quit and relaunch B; the dialog asks again. Click Restart Now: one daemon reporting B (ppid = the GUI), the old shells are gone, `/sessions` is `[]`, the window shows No Open Sessions and no error banner.
  3. CLI: A's CLI starts a daemon with two ticking sessions (no GUI); install B. B's `portty list` exits 1 with `portty: the running daemon is version <A> with 2 live sessions; restarting it to version <B> would end them. Re-run as 'portty <command> --restart-daemon …' to restart anyway, or as 'portty <command> --keep-daemon …' to use it as it is.` and the tickers keep advancing; `portty list --restart-daemon` exits 0 and the daemon is B. With no sessions, B's `portty list --local` restarts an A daemon at once (rc 0).
  4. CLI flags (08-06-REVIEW-FIX WR-04, IN-04): with the A daemon and two sessions, B's `portty list --keep-daemon` lists them with a one-line warning and the A daemon stays (same pid); `portty rename <id> --restart-daemon` exits 2 with the placement message and the tickers keep advancing; `portty health` and `portty version` exit 0 without refusing; `portty --restart-daemon list` runs `list` (no window opens).
  5. Start minimized (08-06-REVIEW-FIX WR-03): with Start Minimized on and the A daemon holding two sessions, launch B; the window and Dock icon appear with the dialog. Repeat with the window hidden by Quit GUI Only before a poller-raised prompt.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-19, build 9c2c9c20: G-01, G-02 and G-03 PASS (steps 1 to 3, before the 08-06 review fixes); then build dcbe999d: G-00 (bare launch) and G-04 to G-06 PASS (steps 4 and 5, after the review fixes). Evidence in .planning/phases/08-debt-cleanup/08-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ two real app builds installed in place over a running daemon, native clicks in the GUI dialog and live PTYs. The decision logic is covered by the Section 4 DEBT-08 rows.
  - _Source:_ 08-06-PLAN.md; 08-VERIFICATION.md follow-up 1 and the user decision of 2026-11-19. Restated for the handoff era in 09-06 (09-CONTEXT "First upgrade from v1.1").

### Category AY — Upgrades Keep Sessions (UPGR-01..UPGR-05)

Rig: the office VM (`portty-test`), two ad-hoc signed builds of one commit that differ only in `-X main.Version=` (A and B; B-fault is B built with `-tags vmpreview`), installed in place at `~/ptest/Portty.app` with `rm -rf` and `ditto` (08-VM-CHECKS.md U-01 method); a v1.1 build for the first-upgrade rows. Sessions get a ticker; `/sessions/<id>/tail` and the viewers show them advancing. Fault injection (`PORTTY_VMPREVIEW_HANDOFF_FAULT=<side>:<step>:exit|pauseN`) only inside the VM. Evidence in .planning/phases/09-upgrades-keep-sessions/09-VM-CHECKS.md.

  2. PASS when: no dialog; the same session ids and shell pids (ppid 1 after A exits); `/health` reports B with `api` 2 and `handoff` 1; the tickers never stop; the full-screen app redraws correctly.

- **M-106** Viewers reconnect with a delta (SC2, UPGR-02).

Evidence in .planning/phases/09-upgrades-keep-sessions/09-VM-CHECKS.md. Re-run 2026-11-20: V-02 **FAIL** at bbb34439 (post-handoff grid flip: 4 of 5 reconnects full, CLI scrollback duplicated), fixed in 50903bee (09-07-FIX2.md); V-02 **PASS** twice at 6ec55a2a (5/5 delta resumes, 0 grid changes, both reconnect orders). Re-run 2026-11-21 at 35aaa035 without the settle window (Phase 10 removed it): V-02r **PASS** twice (5/5 delta resumes, 0 grid lines, no repeated or missing tick, the office attach first in run 1 and the GUI tab first in run 2); evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md.

Re-run 2026-11-20 at dc48674b (after CR-01): V-03b PASS twice (immediate kill and 1.5 s kill; old daemon serving again, viewer never disconnected).

  2. PASS when every session is served by B (same ids, shells alive), `/health` reports B, and viewers resume with no gap.

### Category AZ — Input Path Reliability (INPT-01..INPT-06)

Rig: the two Tart VMs (office `portty-test`, home `portty-test-2`, both ephemeral tailnet nodes: never stop them, never log them out), an ad-hoc signed B build on both (A and B for the upgrade re-run), installed with `rm -rf` and `ditto`. Receivers in office sessions: a slow reader `stty raw -echo; perl -e 'binmode STDIN; binmode STDOUT; while(sysread(STDIN,$b,1024)){syswrite(STDOUT,$b); select(undef,undef,undef,0.02)}' > /tmp/<f>` (about 35-50 KB/s; `pv -L 50k` where installed), or `cat`. Payloads `head -c N /dev/urandom | base64 | tr -d '\n'`, sha256 recorded. Safari under WebDriver ignores real key events, so a browser paste is delivered as a DOM paste event (a DataTransfer) on xterm's textarea. Evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md.

- **M-116** A slow paste keeps every viewer (SC1, INPT-01, INPT-02).
  1. An office session, shared, running the slow reader into a fresh file. Paste a 4,000,000-byte payload (about 80-120 s to drain, over 5x the 15 s heartbeat timeout) from a home GUI peer tab, then from a home browser share viewer, then from the office local tab (one run each).
  2. PASS when, for each: the file reaches the payload size with an equal sha256; the office log has no `heartbeat timeout`, `too slow` or `leave` for that viewer during the drain (and the home daemon no `proxy heartbeat timeout`); there is no reconnecting banner and no paste notice.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build bc86de32: R-01a, R-01b and R-01c PASS (drains of 116.3 s, 117.5 s and 101.8 s, files sha256-equal, no reap, no banner); evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a native Cmd+V into WKWebView and a browser, a live PTY behind a slow reader and a real tailnet between two Macs. Covered in process by input_test.go, server_input_queue_test.go, input_queue_test.go, pong_test.go and remote_ws_proxy_test.go (Section 4 INPT-01/02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 10-03-SUMMARY.md; 10-07-PLAN.md; ROADMAP Phase 10 SC1.
- **M-117** A dead viewer is reaped while its backlog drains (SC1, INPT-01).
  1. An office session, shared, with the slow reader. A home peer tab pastes 6,000,000 bytes; about 15 s later, `kill -STOP` the home daemon (it holds the tab's proxy socket); sample the office file every 0.2 s.
  2. PASS when: within 45 s of the stop the office logs `heartbeat timeout` and `input backlog … bytes=<n>` with n > 0 for that conn while the file is still below the payload size (file + backlog = the bytes read, the backlog counting the frame being written as whole); the file keeps growing after the reap, and `input backlog drained` follows; the final file is an exact prefix whose length equals that conn's `input total` bytes. After `kill -CONT` the tab reconnects with a delta and shows no paste notice.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build 35aaa035: R-05 **PASS** (re-run after the fix): reap about 43 s after the stop with a 4,066,747-byte backlog, all 6,000,000 bytes written (`cmp` equal), rejoin `mode=delta bytes=0`. The first run (build bc86de32) **FAILED** only on the rejoin (`mode=full`: the paste's typing claim had set `geomEnd` with no output after it); fixed in 9cda1853 (10-07-FIX.md). Evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ freezing a real daemon on another Mac. Covered in process by `TestShare_DeadViewerReapedBacklogDelivered` and the replay_fidelity_test.go resume-at-geomEnd cases.
  - _Source:_ 10-03-SUMMARY.md; 10-07-FIX.md; ROADMAP Phase 10 SC1.
- **M-118** A paste that fully arrived shows no notice on a later close (SC2, INPT-02).
  1. An office session, shared, running `stty -icanon echo; cat > /tmp/<f>` (check `echo`, not `-echo`, with `stty -a -f /dev/<tty>`). A home peer tab pastes 5,000,000 bytes; once the file has grown by that much, the home browser viewer pastes another 5,000,000; then stop sharing on the office.
  2. PASS when both viewers show the share-stopped banner and neither shows `Paste may have been cut short` or `Paste interrupted`; the file holds both payloads in order (sha256 of each half).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build 35aaa035: R-02 PASS (both halves sha256-equal, `cmp` equal; only `portty-vm-office stopped sharing this session.` in the GUI tab and in Safari); evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real echo flood through a peer tab and a browser over the tailnet. Covered by pong_test.go and the relayClient.test.ts burst-ack case.
  - _Source:_ 10-03-SUMMARY.md; ROADMAP Phase 10 SC2.
- **M-119** The browser viewer shows the paste notice on a tailnet drop (SC3, INPT-03).
  1. A home browser share viewer on an office session running `stty raw -echo; cat > /tmp/<f>`; paste 5,000,000 bytes; drop the tailnet path the moment the file is non-empty, with either mechanism from M-101 (a silent pf block of the office share port, used from Phase 10 on, or `tailscale down`). Hold the drop until the browser shows the notice (its watchdog is `deadMs` 40 s plus `probeGraceMs` 5 s; up to 90 s with pf, 70 s with `tailscale down`), then lift it.
  2. PASS when the notice text (`Paste may have been cut short (M KB)` or `Paste interrupted: N of M KB sent`, M = ceil(P/1024)) sits in an element with `role="status"`; the page logged no CSP violation and no innerHTML or other error (a `securitypolicyviolation` listener and an error hook installed before the paste); the office file is an exact prefix equal to that conn's `input total`; the viewer reconnects after the lift.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build 35aaa035: R-04 PASS, mechanism pf: the notice appeared 39.1 s into the drop inside `.session-ended-banner` (`role="status"`); 0 CSP violations, 0 errors; the office file (98,301 bytes) was an exact prefix equal to `input total`; rejoin `mode=delta bytes=0`. Evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real browser socket and a real network outage between two Macs. The jsdom half is TerminalPanel.reconnect.test.tsx (Section 4 INPT-03 row).
  - _Source:_ 10-03-SUMMARY.md; 10-CONTEXT.md (INPT-03 target); ROADMAP Phase 10 SC3.
- **M-120** The viewer you are typing on sets the size (SC6, INPT-06).
  1. An office session open in the office local tab and in a home peer tab, with a zsh `TRAPWINCH` that logs `COLS=$(tput cols) LINES=$(tput lines)`. Check each step with `stty -f /dev/<tty> size`, the winch log and the office `viewer-diag: grid … why=` lines.
     - Peer tab: Cmd+Shift+= twice, then resize the home window; type in the office tab; type in the home tab.
     - Browser: a window resize, then browser zoom (View > Zoom In); type in the office tab; type in the browser.
     - `portty attach` (home `portty attach <office>:<session>` and an office-local one): connect, resize its terminal (SIGWINCH), type in the office tab, type in the attach.
     - Idle: every viewer on the session; start `vim` from the current owner; then 60 s with nobody typing.
     - Relaunch: quit the office GUI, relaunch it at a fit different from the grid, then type in it.
     - Alternation: the office and home tabs type alternately five times each.
  2. PASS when:
     - each zoom, window resize and attach resize moves the grid to that viewer's fit (`why=claim`);
     - typing in a viewer that does not own the grid moves it to that viewer's fit (`why=input`), and typing by the owner moves nothing;
     - joining, connecting and clicking move nothing;
     - the idle minute logs no grid line and no reconnect;
     - the relaunch logs no grid line, and its first typing logs exactly one;
     - alternation logs exactly one grid line per hand-over.
  - Accepted limits:
    - a relaunched GUI shows the current grid (scaled) until you type, zoom or resize;
    - `portty attach` can show a grid wider than its terminal until its user types;
    - two viewers typing alternately reflow the shell at each hand-over.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build 35aaa035: R-06a, R-06b, R-06c, R-06d, R-06e and R-06f PASS. Every step logged the expected `why=claim` or `why=input` line with the matching `stty` size and winch `COLS=` line; the idle minute had 0 log lines; 10 hand-overs gave 10 grid lines. Evidence in .planning/phases/10-input-path-reliability/10-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ native key events and window resizes in WKWebView and Safari, a real terminal's SIGWINCH, and terminal query replies from real viewers. Covered by hub_grid_owner_test.go, claim_test.go, oscabsorb_test.go, attach_test.go, attach_viewers_test.go and TerminalPanel.scale.test.tsx (Section 4 INPT-06 rows).
  - _Source:_ the 10-04, 10-05 and 10-06 SUMMARYs; 10-RESEARCH.md Pitfalls 9-11; ROADMAP Phase 10 SC6.
### Category BA — File Preview (PREV-01..PREV-05)

Rig: the two Tart VMs (office `portty-test`, home `portty-test-2`, both ephemeral tailnet nodes: never stop them, never log them out), an ad-hoc signed B build on both (A, B and B2 for the upgrade rows), installed with `rm -rf` and `ditto`; WebGL off in settings.json for the run (backed up, restored with `cmp`). A loopback beacon on 127.0.0.1:8765 in each VM (`perl IO::Socket::INET`, one log line per connection; the VMs have no Command Line Tools, so no python3). Samples in the office VM: PNG, JPEG, GIF (`screencapture`, `sips`), WebP, SVG, PDF (`cupsfilter`), Markdown, the three hostile fixtures with `BEACON` replaced by the beacon address, `near.jpg` (19,900,000 bytes), `over.png` (20,000,001 bytes). Typing into a GUI tab is System Events keystrokes after a click into the terminal (typing makes that viewer the owner the focus rule reads); Safari typing is WebDriver element keys. `screencapture` over SSH does not draw web content in these VMs, so rendering is read from the AX tree (and Safari's DOM through WebDriver). Evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md.

- **M-121** A preview reaches every app viewer; only the typist switches; closing it leaves the terminal (SC1, PREV-01).
  1. An office session open in the office local tab and in a home peer tab. Type `portty open <png>` in the office tab; then `portty open <pdf>` in the home tab; close the home preview with ×; then `portty open <missing>` and `portty open <text file>`. Then keep a preview visible in the home tab for 30 s and switch back.
  2. PASS when: each open adds a preview tab right of the session's tab in both viewers; only the viewer that typed switches to it (the other's active tab is unchanged); × returns to the session tab with the terminal focused and the shell pid unchanged; the missing and unsupported files print exactly `portty: "<path>" doesn't exist.` and `portty: can't preview this file type ("<path>"). …` and open nothing; the office log has `preview accepted` and a per-conn `preview end … status=ok`; returning from the 30 s preview logs no `leave`, `join` or grid line and leaves `stty -f` unchanged.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build f43f20bb (product 7042f5ce): P-01 and P-05 PASS; evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ native typing into WKWebView in two GUIs over a real tailnet, a live PTY and the focus rule across machines. Covered by App.preview.test.tsx, TerminalPanel.preview.test.tsx, preview_test.go (relay and daemon) and cmd_open_test.go (Section 4 PREV-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 11-08-SUMMARY.md (CLI lines); 11-09-SUMMARY.md (tabs, focus, close); ROADMAP Phase 11 SC1.
- **M-122** Every type renders in light and dark (SC2, PREV-02).
  1. Open PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, SVG, PDF and Markdown in that session; show each preview tab in the office local tab and the home peer tab; switch both VMs to dark mode live and show each again; restore light.
  2. PASS when: rasters show with the Actual Size toggle (or at natural size), SVG as one image, PDF in WKWebView's own viewer (`AXPDFPluginSubrole` with its pages), Markdown as an article with headings, lists, a table, code, image placeholders and task glyphs; the same after the dark switch with no reload (no log line, same conn ids); in a browser the Markdown pane repaints dark and the checkerboard stays light. Also: an 81,000-byte `*a **b _c` Markdown file shows Couldn't Show File after about 3 s (the worker limit) while the window stays responsive.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build f43f20bb: P-02 PASS (dark colours checked in Safari, P-04); a damaged PDF shows WKWebView's blank view, not Couldn't Show File (recorded). Evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md; the PDF-in-Wails proof is 11-SPIKE.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real WKWebView and Safari rendering, the native PDF view and a live appearance switch. Covered by PreviewRenderers.test.tsx, MarkdownPreview.test.tsx, markdown.test.ts, parseMarkdownInWorker.test.ts and PreviewPanel.test.tsx (Section 4 PREV-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 11-SPIKE.md; 11-06/11-07 SUMMARYs; 11-07-FIX.md; ROADMAP Phase 11 SC2.
- **M-123** Hostile SVG and Markdown fetch and run nothing (SC2, PREV-02).
  1. With the office local tab, a home peer tab and a home browser share viewer on one session (a `securitypolicyviolation` listener, error hooks and a `window.open` counter installed in the browser), open hostile.md, hostile-script.svg and a hostile external SVG with every network reference at the loopback beacon; show each in every viewer; wait 30 s.
  2. PASS when both beacons log 0 connections beyond their self-tests; window and page titles are unchanged; no new window or tab opened; the browser logged no violation and no error; the Markdown holds no script, style, img, iframe or `on*` attribute and only http/https/mailto/# links; each SVG is one `<img src=blob:>`; typing still works. Note: hostile-external.svg as committed (with its billion-laughs DOCTYPE) is refused by `portty open` as an unsupported type, so its fetch vectors are tested with the same file minus the DOCTYPE block.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build f43f20bb: P-03 PASS (0 beacon connections; 0 violations, 0 errors, 0 `window.open`); evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real network silence from real WKWebView and Safari engines. Covered by PreviewRenderers.test.tsx and the markdown.test.ts and MarkdownPreview.test.tsx hostile-fixture cases.
  - _Source:_ 11-06 and 11-07 SUMMARYs; 11-SECURITY-REVIEW.md; ROADMAP Phase 11 SC2.
- **M-124** The browser share viewer previews under the reviewed CSP (SC4, PREV-04).
  1. A home Safari share viewer on an office session. Type `portty open <png>` in Safari, then a PDF and a Markdown file; click Open PDF; check the tab bar; `curl -skI` the share link and `/app/` from the home VM (host redacted); try a wrong token.
  2. PASS when: Safari switches to its own preview and focuses it; PNG and Markdown render (the parse worker loads same-origin), the PDF pane shows "This browser can't show the PDF inside the page." and Open PDF opens a top-level `blob:` `application/pdf` window; each preview tab has × and no app context menu; the served CSP equals 11-SECURITY-REVIEW.md "After (Amendment 3)" with `frame-ancestors 'none'` last; a wrong token gets 401; 0 CSP violations.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build f43f20bb: P-04 PASS; evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real Safari, its PDF viewer and the served CSP over a real share link. Covered by csp_mw_test.go and csp_integration_test.go (Section 4 PREV-04 rows) and PreviewRenderers.test.tsx (Open PDF).
  - _Source:_ 11-SPIKE.md; 11-SECURITY-REVIEW.md; ROADMAP Phase 11 SC4.
- **M-125** A near-cap preview keeps every viewer and the terminal; over-cap is refused; no preview route elsewhere (SC3, PREV-03).
  1. An office session with a ticker writing a numbered line every 200 ms, viewed by the office local tab, a home peer tab, a home browser viewer and a home `portty attach <office>:<session>`. `portty open near.jpg` (19,900,000 bytes) from the attach; sample each viewer's tick numbers before, during and after. Then `portty open over.png` (20,000,001 bytes). Probe POST and GET `/sessions/<id>/preview` and `/app/sessions/<id>/preview` on the share server with a valid token, and POST and GET `/sessions/<id>/preview` on the office loopback relay port.
  2. PASS when: one `preview end … status=ok` per viewer (with its elapsed time) and no `leave`, `too slow` or heartbeat line; every viewer's ticks advance throughout and `seqcheck.sh` finds no gap or duplicate; each GUI and the browser render near.jpg; over.png prints `portty: "<path>" is too large to preview (20.0 MB; the limit is 20 MB).` in every viewer and opens nothing (no `preview accepted`); every probe answers 404, 405 or 401, except `GET /app/sessions/<id>/preview`, which is the `/app/*` single-page-app fallback and must be byte-identical to `GET /app/`.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build f43f20bb: P-06 and P-07 PASS (stream times: office local 168 ms, home peer tab 14.95 s, home attach 19.37 s, Safari 19.41 s over DERP); evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a 20 MB stream over a real tailnet to four kinds of viewer with a live PTY. Covered by `TestHubSendPreview_NearCapNoKick`, `TestPreview_NotOnRelayHandler`, `TestPreviewRoute_NotOnShareServer` and cmd_open_test.go (Section 4 PREV-03 rows).
  - _Source:_ 11-02 and 11-04 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 11 SC3.
- **M-126** `portty` on the PATH and Install Command-Line Tool… (SC5, PREV-05).
  1. Install the app to `/Applications` and launch it from there. In a new Portty session (zsh login, nothing added by hand): `command -v portty` and `portty open <png>`. In Terminal.app: `command -v portty; echo rc=$?`. Choose Portty ▸ Install Command-Line Tool…, enter the password, then in Terminal.app `readlink /usr/local/bin/portty` and `portty list`. Remove the link and choose the item again, then Don't Allow. Make `/usr/local/bin/portty` a link to `/bin/echo` and choose the item; point it at the app and choose it again. Launch a disk-image copy of the app and choose the item. From SSH with no session env, run `<app binary> open --help`.
  2. PASS when: the session resolves `portty` to `~/Library/Application Support/portty/bin/portty` (a link into the running app) and the preview opens; Terminal.app prints rc=1 before the install; the install shows the success dialog, links `/usr/local/bin/portty` to `/Applications/Portty.app/Contents/MacOS/Portty`, and Terminal.app's `portty list` exits 0; Don't Allow shows no dialog and creates nothing; the foreign link gets the "Another portty command is already installed." warning naming its target with no prompt and stays unchanged; the app link gets "The portty command is already installed."; the disk-image copy gets "Move Portty to the Applications folder first." naming `/Volumes/<vol>`; `open --help` prints the usage, exits 0 and starts no GUI or daemon. Note: macOS 27.2's prompt shows its own copy ("Allow administrator access for a script started by “Portty”?") and not the app's `with prompt` text.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build f43f20bb: P-08a, P-08b, P-08c, P-08d, P-08e, P-08f and P-10 PASS. In P-08c the user typed the VM password into the administrator prompt over Screen Sharing; afterwards `/usr/local/bin/portty` was a root link to `/Applications/Portty.app/Contents/MacOS/Portty`, and in a clean `env -i` login zsh `command -v portty` gave `/usr/local/bin/portty` and `portty list` exited 0. The success dialog's exact text was not captured. Evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the macOS administrator prompt, Terminal.app, `/Applications` and a disk-image launch. Covered by clilink_test.go (including a real zsh login shell), app_cli_install_test.go, menu_test.go and cmd_open_test.go (Section 4 PREV-05 rows).
  - _Source:_ 11-03 and 11-08 SUMMARYs; 11-UI-SPEC.md (dialogs); ROADMAP Phase 11 SC5.
- **M-127** Upgrades keep sessions with previews in play (UPGR, PREV-01).
  1. Office A with a ticking session viewed by the office local tab, a home peer tab, a home browser viewer and a home `portty attach`. In that session run B's `portty open <png>` with the session id set by hand; then upgrade with B's `portty list`; then run B's `portty open` in the moved session and plain `portty open` in a new session. Next, office B with a session viewed from home (peer tab and browser): start `portty open near.jpg` and, while the home conns still receive, run B2's `portty list`. Last, home on A with an A peer tab on an office B session: `portty open near.jpg`.
  2. PASS when: the first open prints `portty: update needed. Portty's background service (version <A>) can't show previews; relaunch Portty to update it.` and A keeps running; after the upgrade every viewer is back within 10 s with `mode=delta`, ticks gapless, 0 grid lines, no dialog; the moved session prints `portty: this shell isn't a Portty session. …` and the new session's preview opens; the handoff during the preview commits, every viewer reconnects with `mode=delta`, each home viewer finished the preview or shows Couldn't Receive File (none stuck on Receiving), and a later `portty open` works; the A peer tab stays connected through the near-cap stream (no `leave`, `too slow` or heartbeat line), keeps ticking, and shows no error and no tab.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build f43f20bb with A at 7dbe384e: U-01, U-02 and U-03 PASS (U-01: 4 of 4 delta within 2.1 s; U-02: 3 of 3 delta within 4.5 s, both home viewers Couldn't Receive File); evidence in .planning/phases/11-file-preview/11-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real two-build upgrades of a running daemon with live viewers on two Macs. Covered by `TestPreview_HandoffDrainNotBlocked`, `TestHubSendPreview_HubEnds` and the API pins in api_test.go and client_test.go.
  - _Source:_ 11-04-SUMMARY.md (API 3, freeze gate); 09-VM-CHECKS.md V-02; ROADMAP Phase 11 Depends on.

### Category BB — File Path Click (PATH-01..PATH-02)

Rig: the two Tart VMs (office `portty-test`, home `portty-test-2`, both ephemeral tailnet nodes: never stop them, never log them out), an ad-hoc signed build on both (C and C2 for the upgrade row), installed with `rm -rf` and `ditto`; WebGL off in settings.json for the run (backed up, restored with `cmp`). Samples in the office VM `~/p12/proj/docs`: `diagram.png` (a screen capture, 28 KB), `readme.md`, `notes.txt`, `big.dat` (20,000,001 bytes, sparse), `list.txt`, `sub/`. Print paths with the glob form `clear; echo; ls -1d docs/*` (a blank line first keeps the path off screen row 0, see M-128). Clicks and drags are real CGEvents (`~/p12/p12ui`), no System Events; Safari clicks are the synthetic Meta DOM sequence through WebDriver (Safari ignores real HID events). Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md.

- **M-128** A Cmd-click on a printed path previews it in a local tab, a peer tab and a browser viewer; only the clicker switches (SC2, PATH-02).
  1. An office session `cd ~/p12/proj` with the zsh shim, open in the office local tab, a home peer tab and a home Safari share viewer. The office types `clear; echo; ls -1d docs/*`. Cmd-click `docs/diagram.png` in the office tab; close the previews; repeat in the home peer tab (the office typed last) and in Safari. Also: a plain click, an absolute path (`ls -d ~/p12/proj/docs/readme.md`) and `echo https://xn--exmple-cua.com/docs` with a Cmd-click in the office tab and in the peer tab (Cancel the popover).
  2. PASS when: each Cmd-click logs `path click … outcome=preview` with the clicker's conn; the clicker switches to its new preview tab and every other viewer gets a background tab; a plain click opens nothing and logs nothing; the absolute path previews as Markdown; the URL opens the "Confirm link destination" popover in both desktop tabs and no browser starts.
  - Accepted limits: a relative path printed on screen row 0 of a never-scrolled session resolves against the shell's start directory (F-1, `outcome=missing`; user decision 2026-11-22, "Todos, not blockers": .planning/todos/pending/2026-11-22-row0-relative-path-start-dir.md); keep the path off row 0 when checking.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build C (v1.2.0-vmC, product from 3a8972a6): C-01R, C-02R and C-03R PASS (office, home peer tab with conn=2, and Safari each `outcome=preview`, only the clicker switched; the peer tab's URL Cmd-click opened the popover). Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md (first run C-01, C-03 PASS; C-02 FAIL superseded by C-02R).
  - _Why not automatable:_ native Cmd-click into WKWebView and Safari, a live PTY and a real tailnet peer. Covered by pathLinks.linkifier.test.ts, WebShareSessionView.peerLinks.test.tsx, TerminalPanel.pathClick.test.tsx and the relay and share-server path-click tests (Section 4 PATH-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 12-06, 12-08 and 12-09 SUMMARYs; 12-VERIFICATION.md gap 1; ROADMAP Phase 12 SC2.
- **M-129** A missing path or folder is silent; notices appear only in the clicking window (SC3, PATH-02).
  1. As M-128 with the three viewers. In turn from the office tab, home Safari and the home peer tab, Cmd-click `docs/notes.txt`, `docs/big.dat`, a missing path (`echo docs/missing.png`) and a folder (`ls -d ~/p12/proj/docs/sub`, absolute); hash the session tail before and after each.
  2. PASS when: `docs/notes.txt` shows exactly "Can't preview this file type. Portty previews images, PDFs and Markdown files." and `docs/big.dat` exactly "Too large to preview. The limit is 20 MB.", each only in the clicking window and gone after about 5 s; the missing path and the folder log `outcome=missing`, show nothing anywhere and open no tab; the session tail is unchanged (nothing typed into the shell).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build C: C-04R PASS in all three clicking viewers (peer tab conn=2 `unsupported` and `too-large` with the exact copy only in the home GUI; office and Safari polls empty; missing and folder silent, tail sha256 unchanged). Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md (first run C-04 FAIL superseded by C-04R).
  - _Why not automatable:_ the notice in real GUIs and Safari beside other live viewers over a real tailnet. Covered by SessionEndedBanner.test.tsx, TerminalPanel.pathClick.test.tsx and `TestPathClick_Outcomes` (Section 4 PATH-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 12-04 and 12-06 SUMMARYs; 12-UI-SPEC.md (notices); ROADMAP Phase 12 SC3.
- **M-130** A read-only viewer gets no path link and its hand-sent click is refused (SC4, PATH-02).
  1. Mint a read-only capability for a live share (a throwaway signer inside the office VM, never committed; the key never leaves the VM) and open it in home Safari. Try typing; Meta-hover along a printed path; send `0x13` plus the printed path on the page's own WebSocket; then have a writable viewer click the same path.
  2. PASS when: typing logs `first input … passed=0` and the session tail is unchanged; the cursor stays `text` along the path with no underline; the hand-sent frame leaves no `path click` line for that conn, no preview and no notice anywhere; the writable viewer's click then reaches the read-only viewer as a background preview tab (accepted risk, as `portty open`).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build C: C-05R PASS (read-only conn=5: `passed=0`, 12 Meta-hover positions all `text`, hand-sent 0x13 gave 0 log lines; the writable click then showed a background preview tab in the read-only Safari). Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md (first run C-05 PASS).

- **M-131** The path underline follows Cmd, and a showing notice repaints in dark mode (UI-SPEC, PATH-02).
  1. On your Mac, in the desktop app with a local tab: print a path, hold Cmd over it without moving, then move, then release without moving. Make `docs/notes.txt` show its notice and switch the appearance to dark while it shows. In Safari on a share link, the same underline and dark switch.
  2. PASS when: the underline and pointer cursor appear on the path while Cmd is held (also with no mouse move) and go away on release; the notice repaints dark with no reload. Safari: `.xterm-screen` cursor `text`, `pointer` after a Meta keydown with no move, `text` after the keyup; the notice reads `rgb(31, 42, 58)` background and `rgb(156, 203, 255)` text in dark.

The underline stays Cmd-held; UI-SPEC's hover fallback was not chosen. Check this on your own Macs.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build C: C-06R PASS in Safari (the cursor followed Meta with no move; the notice repainted dark in 0.9 s on the same conn with no `join` or `leave`). The desktop-app half is open for your Macs. Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md (first run C-06 recorded PASS by the user's decision, Task 3 UI-2).
  - _Why not automatable:_ real WKWebView key events and the live appearance switch on a real Mac. Covered by pathLinks.test.ts ("underline follows the modifier") and TerminalPanel.pathClick.test.tsx.

- **M-132** A path click works in a mouse-reporting TUI and types nothing (UI-SPEC, PATH-02).
  1. In the office local tab: `vim -c "set mouse=a" docs/list.txt` (the file names `docs/diagram.png`). Cmd-click the path in vim; `:q`; Cmd-click a path in the shell. Then, on your Mac, the same inside Claude Code.
  2. PASS when: the click logs `outcome=preview`; the session tail has 0 SGR mouse reports (`<N;N;NM`) from the click; vim quits cleanly and the shell takes the next command; the shell click previews too.
