  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build C: C-07R PASS (vim 9.1 `mouse=a`: `path click … conn=1 outcome=preview`, 0 SGR mouse reports before and after, `:q` clean, shell click previewed). Claude Code not run. Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md (first run C-07 PASS).
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real TUI with mouse tracking on a live PTY and a real Claude Code. Covered by the `pathLinks.linkifier.test.ts` zero-input test (no `onData` from the link refresh with mouse tracking on) and the hub tests in Section 4.
  - _Source:_ 12-04 and 12-06 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 12 SC2 backstops.
- **M-133** Shell startup is unchanged with the zsh shim (SC1, PATH-01).
  1. On your Mac, in a new Portty session: compare `PROMPT`, aliases, `$ZDOTDIR`, `$HISTFILE` and `command -v portty` against a plain login zsh, with and without a `~/.zshenv` that exports `ZDOTDIR` and an rc (oh-my-zsh) that empties `chpwd_functions`; `x=$(cd /tmp && pwd)`; `zsh -i -c`; `portty open docs/diagram.png`; then `ssh localhost` (or another host), run `pwd` and exit.
  2. PASS when: every value equals the plain login zsh's; `$(cd …)` captures no escape byte; the nested zsh starts; the relative `portty open` previews; `ssh localhost` reaches a remote shell and returns; the log has no `zsh shim:` line.
  - Accepted limit (user decision 2026-11-22, "Check on my Macs"): `ssh localhost` could not be exercised in the VMs (the VM user has no key for itself; copying the rig key or typing the VM password was not allowed). Check it on your own Macs. A relative click after a remote `cd` resolves against the outer directory (the documented limit in 12-SECURITY-REVIEW.md).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build B (first run; no 12-08 change touches the shim or the host, so C-08 was not re-run): C-08 PASS in both dotfile setups for every clause except `ssh localhost` (UI-1, your Macs). Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the user's real dotfiles, oh-my-zsh and an ssh login. Covered by the `TestZshShim_*` tests, including `TestZshShim_CleanStartupNoErrors` on the real `/bin/zsh` (Section 4 PATH-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 12-02 SUMMARY; 12-SECURITY-REVIEW.md "Shell integration"; ROADMAP Phase 12 SC1.
- **M-134** A printed path stays clickable after a resize and a grid claim; the wrap limits hold (UI-SPEC, PATH-02).
  1. Office local tab, `clear; echo; ls -1d docs/*`. Drag the window's right edge narrower, Cmd-click `docs/diagram.png`, close the preview (Cmd-W), drag wider, then Cmd-click at the same point with no typing and no other pointer move. Then rest the pointer on the path and have the home peer tab claim the grid (resize its window narrower) and click the same point, then restore and click again. Also a 137-character path that wraps (click both rows, on screen and after scrolling into history), an identical hard row above it, and `printf 'docs/dia\ngram.png\n'`.
  2. PASS when: every same-point Cmd-click logs `path click … outcome=preview` (a miss with `outcome=not-printed` is the host refusing, no line is the viewer not sending); both wrapped rows preview, on screen and in history; the identical hard row is `not-printed`; neither fragment of the hard break sends anything (accepted limit, 2026-11-21).
  - Accepted limits: after the pointer leaves the terminal and returns to the same cell whose text changed meanwhile, the first Cmd-click sends nothing until another cell is crossed (F-2; user decision 2026-11-22, "Todos, not blockers": .planning/todos/pending/2026-11-22-first-cmd-click-same-cell-after-leave.md); hard-wrapped TUI paths and paths split by a real newline are never clickable.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build C: C-09R PASS (narrow, widen, grid claim by the home tab and restore each gave `outcome=preview` at the same point; wrap rows 0 and 1, history, identical-row and hard-break clauses as specified). The plan's Cmd+= does not change the grid (the zoom key is Shift+=), so the claim was made by resizing the home window. Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md (first run C-09 FAIL superseded by C-09R).
  - _Why not automatable:_ real window drags, a real renderer's cell geometry and a second viewer's grid claim over a tailnet. Covered by pathLinks.linkifier.test.ts (reconnect and resize, real xterm Linkifier) and the relay wrap tests (Section 4 PATH-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 12-04, 12-06 and 12-08 SUMMARYs; 12-VERIFICATION.md gap 2; ROADMAP Phase 12 SC2 backstops.
- **M-135** Upgrades keep the cwd log and surviving viewers keep their links (UPGR, PATH-01, PATH-02).
  1. Office on an old build with a session made by it (no shim), viewed by three viewers; upgrade with the new build's `portty list`; click an absolute path (previews) and a relative one (`no-cwd`); make a new session under the new build and click a relative path. Then session u2 made by C, `cd ~/p12/proj; clear; ls -1d docs/*`, viewed by the office tab, a home peer tab and a Safari page loaded before the upgrade; each clicks once and closes its preview, the pointer left resting; upgrade C to C2; then, with nothing typed and no pointer move, each viewer Cmd-clicks the same point.
  2. PASS when: every viewer is back within 10 s with `mode=delta` and 0 `viewer-diag: grid` lines; no dialog; the old session's absolute path previews and its relative path gets `no-cwd`; the new session resolves relative paths; all three post-upgrade first clicks log `path click … outcome=preview` with the post-upgrade conn ids.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, builds C to C2: U-01R PASS (handoff in 87 ms; 3 of 3 `mode=delta` within 1.62 s; 0 grid lines; the office tab (conn=1), the home peer tab (conn=2) and the same Safari page (conn=3) each previewed on the first click). The A to B clauses are from the first run, U-01 (A to B: 3 of 3 delta within 1.9 s; absolute previews, relative `no-cwd`, new session resolves), carried because 12-08 did not touch the host. Evidence in .planning/phases/12-file-path-click/12-VM-CHECKS.md (first run U-01 FAIL superseded by U-01R).
  - _Why not automatable:_ two-build upgrades of a running daemon with live viewers on two Macs and a long-lived browser page. Covered by `TestHandoffState_CwdRoundTrip`, `TestHandoffState_CwdValidates` and pathLinks.linkifier.test.ts (reconnect).
  - _Source:_ 12-03 and 12-08 SUMMARYs; 12-VERIFICATION.md gap 2; ROADMAP Phase 12 Depends on.

### Category BC — Selection and Copy (debug selection-offset-copy)

Rig: Tart VM `portty-test` (never stop it), the desktop app built from the fix commit.

- **M-136** A drag selects the row under the pointer on a scaled pane, and Option-drag + Cmd-C copies from Claude Code fullscreen.
  1. In the desktop app, open a session that follows a grid larger than the pane (a second viewer claimed a bigger grid, or toggle the sidebar after the grid was set), so `term.element.style.transform` is `scale(s)` with s < 1. Fill the screen with numbered lines and drag across a row near the bottom. Then start Claude Code with `"tui": "fullscreen"`, Option-drag across a line of its output and press Cmd-C; paste into another app.
  2. PASS when: the highlight covers the row under the pointer (top, middle and bottom rows) with s < 1; under Claude Code a plain drag is Claude Code's own and lands on the pointer row; the Option-drag shows an xterm selection on the pointer row and the paste is exactly that text.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-21, build v1.2.0-selfix from 7a37bfb4 in `portty-test`: PASS with a mouse-tracking TUI stand-in (ESC[?1002h ESC[?1006h, the modes Claude Code fullscreen sets; Claude Code is not installed in the VM). Scale 0.803 (desktop fit 87x29 following a 109x36 grid an attach claimed): drags on rows 3/18/34 copied row-3/row-18/row-34 (the pre-fix build copied row-2/row-15/row-27); the TUI got row 34 for a drag on row 34 (pre-fix: 27); Option-drags copied the pointer row and sent the TUI nothing (pre-fix: alt-modified reports, nothing copied). Scale 1: rows 3/15/27 exact. A real Claude Code fullscreen run is still open.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real WKWebView, the native Edit menu's Cmd-C, a real Claude Code fullscreen TUI and the macOS pasteboard. Covered by frontend/e2e/selection-offset.spec.ts and terminalScale.test.ts (Section 4 VIEW-05 rows).
  - _Source:_ .planning/debug/resolved/selection-offset-copy.md.

### Category BD — Remote Localhost URLs (URL-01..URL-03)

Rig: the two Tart VMs (office `portty-test`, home `portty-test-2`, both ephemeral tailnet nodes: never stop them, never log them out, never `tailscale down`), ad-hoc signed builds A13 (`9574fd49`, API 4), B13 (v1.2.0-vm13b, API 5) and HEAD, installed with `rm -rf` and `ditto`; WebGL off in settings.json for the run (backed up, restored with `cmp`). The office is the host Mac and shares the sessions; the home VM is the viewing Mac whose peer tab is clicked and whose default browser (Safari) opens the pages. Helper `p13dev` (built on the host, never committed): `-static` file server, `-dev` page with a WebSocket echo, an SSE stream and a reload on `~/p13/hot.txt`, and `-trip` TCP tripwire. Links, capabilities and port tokens only in 0600 files in deleted private directories.

- **M-137** A Cmd-click on a printed `localhost` URL in a home peer tab opens the office page, its assets, a subfolder and a query string in the home default browser through Portty (SC1, URL-01).
  1. An office session shared and open in a home peer tab. In it run `p13dev -static ~/p13/site -addr :8000` (the banner prints `http://[::]:8000/`) and Cmd-click the banner's URL, Allow. Then `echo 'http://127.0.0.1:8000/sub/page.html?q=a%20b'` and Cmd-click it. Check Safari's front window, the server's request log and `lsof` on the home VM.
  2. PASS when: the sheet appears once; Safari's title is `p13 site` at address `localhost:8000`; the server log shows `GET /`, `/app.js` and `/logo.png` each 200 with `host=localhost:8000`; the second click opens with no sheet, Safari's title is `p13 sub` and the server saw `/sub/page.html?q=a%20b` exactly; the home listener belongs to the Portty daemon and Safari's WebKit networking process is its client; no new Portty tab or web view opens.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, builds B13 and HEAD: L-02 PASS (static server banner `Serving HTTP on :: port 8000 (http://[::]:8000/) ...`; title `p13 site`, then `p13 sub`; target byte-exact). Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real default browser, real loopback listeners and a real tailnet peer. Covered by portforward_e2e_test.go, portproxy_test.go, TerminalPanel.remotePort.test.tsx and localhostUrl.test.ts (Section 4 URL-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 13-02, 13-03 and 13-05 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 13 SC1.
- **M-138** The consent sheet names the Mac and port with focus on Don't Allow, approval covers only that Mac and port, and the home listener is loopback-only (SC2, URL-02).
  1. As M-137 with a dev server on 8001 printed in the session. Cmd-click it: read the sheet's title, body and buttons; press Return, then click again and press Esc; then click again and Allow. Run `lsof -nP -iTCP:8001 -sTCP:LISTEN` on the home VM. Cmd-click an already approved URL on another port.
  2. PASS when: the title is exactly `Allow access to portty-vm-office's localhost:8001?` and the body exactly `Pages from this port open in your browser through Portty over your tailnet. Other ports stay closed.`; Return and Esc open nothing and bind nothing; Allow opens the page; `lsof` lists exactly `127.0.0.1:8001` and `[::1]:8001` for the daemon and no `*:8001`; the other approved port opens with no sheet while 8001 needed its own.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, build B13: L-03 PASS (Return and Esc left Safari unchanged and `lsof` empty; Allow listed the two loopback listeners only). Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md. The focused element has no AX attribute in the VM rig, so focus was shown by Return opening nothing.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real sheet in the Wails web view and `lsof` on a real Mac. Covered by TerminalPanel.remotePort.test.tsx ("consent sheet") and portforward_test.go (Section 4 URL-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 13-02 and 13-05 SUMMARYs; 13-CONTEXT.md (Consent); ROADMAP Phase 13 SC2.
- **M-139** Refusals come before any connection, a scan learns nothing, and the share port is tailnet-only (SC3, URL-02).
  1. Move the session's share link to the home VM in a 0600 file. With `curl` (headers read from files, nothing printed) request a port grant for a printed port (a positive control), then for no capability, a garbage capability, a port never printed with a tripwire on it, a closed port, 7444 and the office daemon's relay port; then the proxy route with no port token, with the wrong port's token, and with the right one. Curl the office's LAN address on 7444 from the home VM and run `lsof` for 7444 on the office.
  2. PASS when: every refusal is a 403 with `X-Portty: 1` and byte-identical headers (except Date) and body; the tripwire count stays 0; the positive controls return 200; the LAN request fails to connect (curl exit 7) and the office lists 7444 only on its tailnet address.
  - Accepted limit (user decision 2026-11-22, UI-1, "Accept automated-only"): another tailnet user cannot be shown with one account; it is pinned by `TestPortGrant_Refusals` and `TestPortProxy_Refusals` (other-user rows) and needs no manual run.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, build B13: L-04 PASS (six refusals with one normalised hash; tripwire 0 at the end; LAN curl exit 7). Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real tailnet listener and a second Mac's LAN path. Covered by portproxy_test.go and portforward_e2e_test.go (Section 4 URL-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 13-03 and 13-06 SUMMARYs; 13-SECURITY-REVIEW.md; ROADMAP Phase 13 SC3.
- **M-140** A WebSocket, an SSE stream and a hot reload work through the proxy, also for a `::1`-only server, and a real Vite or webpack project's HMR works on your own Macs (SC1, URL-01).
  1. VM part: `p13dev -dev -addr :8100` printed in the session; Cmd-click, Allow; read Safari's title at +2 s and +10 s; `touch ~/p13/hot.txt` on the office. Repeat with `-addr '[::1]:8200'`. Your Macs: with a real Vite or webpack project's dev server on the other Mac, click its printed URL and edit a source file.
  2. PASS when: the title shows `ws:<n> sse:<m> load:1` with both counters rising; the touch gives `load:2` within 5 s and the WebSocket request line shows `host=localhost:8100 origin=http://localhost:8100 portty-hdr=no fwd-hdr=no`; the `::1`-only page counts too; on your Macs the HMR edit appears without a manual reload.
  - Accepted unverified in the VMs (user decision 2026-11-22, UI-2, "Check on my Macs"): neither VM has node, so no real Vite or webpack project was run. Check it on your own Macs and report the result.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, builds B13 and HEAD: L-05 PASS with `p13dev` (counters `ws:3 sse:4` to `ws:12 sse:12`; reload to `load:2` in about 1.2 s; the `::1`-only page counted, its first load slower than the IPv4 pages). Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md. The real-project half is open for your Macs.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real browser, a real dev-server project and a real tailnet. Covered by `TestPortForwardE2E_WebSocketAndSSE`, `TestPortProxy_PathAndHeaders` and `TestPortProxy_DialOrder` (Section 4 URL-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 13-03 and 13-06 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 13 SC1.
- **M-141** Stopping the share ends the open page's streams and closes the forward with no request on the viewing Mac, a re-share asks consent again, and the no-stream window proxies nothing (SC3, URL-02).
  1. A page open in home Safari with counters rising and the home peer tab open. On the office run `portty unshare <session>` and touch nothing on the home VM. Then `portty share <session>`, reopen the peer tab from the sidebar, Cmd-click the printed URL. Last, with the page open again and the peer tab closed, unshare then share on the office and only read the home VM.
  2. PASS when: within 5 s the title shows `ws:closed sse:closed`, `lsof` on home is empty and the home log has `port forward: … outcome=revoked`; the peer tab shows `portty-vm-office stopped sharing this session.`; the next click shows the consent sheet; in the no-stream window the office dev server logs no request after the unshare, the first request (the page's own retry) logs `outcome=stopped` and `lsof` is empty within 5 s of it, and a click after reopening the tab asks for consent.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, build B13: L-06 PASS (`outcome=revoked` 50 ms after the unshare, streams closed within 0.7 s; the no-stream forward stayed about 4.1 s until the page's retry, then `outcome=stopped` and closed within 0.6 s; the office log unchanged). Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real tailnet, a real browser's retry timing and live GUIs. Covered by `TestPortForwardE2E_StopSharing`, `TestPortForwardE2E_ReshareWithoutStream` and `TestPortProxyRevoke_*` (Section 4 URL-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 13-02 and 13-06 SUMMARYs; 13-SECURITY-REVIEW.md (accepted risk: the no-stream window); ROADMAP Phase 13 SC3.
- **M-142** A port already in use on the viewing Mac is refused and the local server keeps answering (URL-02).
  1. Run a server on the home VM on 8500 (all interfaces) and another on `[::1]:8501`; print `http://localhost:8500/` and then `http://localhost:8501/` in the office session; Cmd-click each in the home peer tab. Curl each home server afterwards.
  2. PASS when: each click shows exactly `Port 8500 is in use on this Mac.` and `Port 8501 is in use on this Mac.` with no sheet; the home log has `outcome=port-in-use`; the home servers still answer and `lsof` lists only them.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, build B13: L-07 PASS. Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real listeners and the notice in the real GUI. Covered by `TestPortOpen_PortInUse` and `TestPortOpen_NoApprovalAfterPortInUse` (Section 4 URL-01 and URL-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 13-02 and 13-05 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 13 gates.
- **M-143** An office on the pre-phase build says it needs an update, and after its upgrade the pre-upgrade URL asks for consent (URL-01).
  1. Office on A13 (API 4) with a shared session running a dev server; the home VM on B13 with a peer tab. Cmd-click the printed URL. Then upgrade the office by handoff (`~/p907/upg.sh <tag> B13 cli`) and, with nothing re-typed, Cmd-click the same URL after moving the pointer to another cell.
  2. PASS when: the first click shows exactly `portty-vm-office needs a Portty update to open its local pages.` with no sheet, nothing bound, no browser and `outcome=host-outdated`; after the upgrade the session survives, the tab reconnects with `mode=delta`, and the same URL shows the consent sheet (`outcome=consent`); Don't Allow opens nothing.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, builds A13 and B13: L-01 PASS. Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ two real builds, a real handoff and a real peer tab. Covered by `TestPortOpen_ErrorCodes` and `TestOpenRemotePort_DaemonOutdated` (Section 4 URL-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 13-02, 13-04 and 13-05 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 13 gates.
- **M-144** Local and non-localhost links behave as before (SC4, URL-03).
  1. In the office local tab with a dev server on 8600: `echo http://localhost:8600/` and Cmd-click. Then `echo https://xn--exmple-cua.com/docs` and Cmd-click it in the office local tab and in the home peer tab. In the home peer tab also `echo https://example.com/` and `echo https://localhost:8600/` and Cmd-click each.
  2. PASS when: the local click opens office Safari at `localhost:8600` with no sheet and no `port forward:` line; the punycode link opens the `link-confirm-popover` in both tabs and Cancel closes it; `https://example.com/` opens in home Safari with no dialog; `https://localhost:8600/` asks home Safari for its own `https://localhost:8600` with no sheet and no forward (the recorded gap).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, build B13: L-08 PASS. Evidence in .planning/phases/13-remote-localhost-urls/13-VM-CHECKS.md.

  - _Source:_ 13-05 SUMMARY; 13-SECURITY-REVIEW.md (accepted risks); ROADMAP Phase 13 SC4.

### Category BE — Mobile Web App (MOB-01..MOB-05)

Rig: the two Tart VMs of Category BD (office `portty-test` is the host Mac, home `portty-test-2` the viewing Mac; ephemeral tailnet nodes, never stop them, never log them out, never `tailscale down`), HEAD build `v1.2.0-vm14` and the `vmwhois` build `v1.2.0-vm14w` (ad-hoc signed, W14 only on the office for the other-account rows); a headless iOS 26.5 simulator (iPhone 17, throwaway device, the Playwright fixture served on loopback with its own CA, a throwaway XCUITest runner, Simulator.app never opened); Playwright's WebKit iPhone 15 emulation; and the user's real iPhone with the Tailscale app on the same account. Links, capabilities and tokens only in 0600 files in deleted private directories. The security contract and the CSP sign-off are in .planning/phases/14-mobile-web-app/14-SECURITY-REVIEW.md.

- **M-145** Home Screen install opens the exact link full screen, every asset from the host, and the icon shows Link No Longer Works after the share stops; Open PDF has a way back in the Home Screen app (SC2, MOB-02).
  1. With the Tailscale app connected on the same account, open a shared session's link in Safari on the iPhone. Tap More, Share, View More, Add to Home Screen, leave Open as Web App on, name Portty, Add. Note where the icon lands (a Home Screen page, or only the App Library's Webclips folder). Close Safari, tap the icon. Then, on the Mac, stop sharing the session and tap the icon again. Share again (a new link): the old icon does not revive; add a new one. In a session with an Open PDF preview, open it in the Home Screen app and tap Done.
  2. PASS when: the icon opens the session full screen with no Safari address bar, the live output and the key bar (both the `session` and `cap` query parameters of the link survived the launch); after the share stops the icon shows Link No Longer Works and stays dead after a re-share; Done returns from the PDF layer without a relaunch.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22. Early spike H-01 PASS and the spike decision "continue" (14-SIM-CHECKS.md); simulator S-04 PASS (info route hit once more by the icon launch), S-05 PASS (safe areas, light and dark), S-06 PASS (Open PDF layer, Done returns, no relaunch), S-07 PASS (Link No Longer Works after share-off, the icon relaunch the same); real iPhone PASS (user, PHONE-1): the page loaded in "less then 5 sec", the icon landed "on the home page" (a Home Screen page), the standalone launch kept the query (full screen, ticks counting, key bar shown). iOS version not reported. Real-iPhone Open PDF and re-share-then-add-again were not run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ SpringBoard, Safari's share sheet and the Home Screen web app process. Covered by homeScreen.test.ts, TestCSPHeaders_ManifestDelta, web-csp.spec.ts, TestAppStatic_CacheHeaders and PreviewRenderers.test.tsx (Section 4 MOB-02 rows).
  - _Source:_ 14-05, 14-07, 14-08 and 14-13 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 14 SC2; 14-SECURITY-REVIEW.md (Home Screen token storage).
- **M-146** The key bar, the keyboard inset and plain ASCII typing work on a phone, and the desktop browser view is unchanged (SC1, MOB-01).
  1. In Safari on the iPhone (and in the Home Screen app) tap the terminal so the keyboard appears. Look at where the key bar sits against the keyboard. Tap Esc, Tab, Ctrl then c, an arrow and the pipe. Type two hyphens, a double quote and two spaces. Rotate to landscape. Open the same link in a desktop browser.
  2. PASS when: the key bar and the cursor line stay visible above the keyboard in both orientations; Esc is `1b`, Tab `09`, Ctrl then c `03` with Ctrl returning to off; the typed text arrives as plain ASCII (no smart punctuation); focusing does not zoom the page; the desktop view shows no key bar.
  - Accepted limit (user decision 2026-11-22, S-02): the key bar does not sit within 8 pt of the keyboard's top edge (117 pt above it in a Safari tab, 85 pt in the Home Screen app, 68 pt in landscape in the simulator); the user's words: "the gap is iOS's own form-assist bar (⌃ ⌄ ✓), which iOS shows above the keyboard for any web text field even in the standalone app; Portty's key bar sits directly on top of it (confirmed in the user's real-iPhone screenshot, standalone)".
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22. Simulator S-01 PASS (eight keys 51x44 pt or larger in one row; `1b`, `03`, `612d2d`, `22`, `622020`), S-02 accepted limit (above), S-05 PASS; Playwright iPhone rows I-01..I-11 PASS; VM P-06 PASS (desktop unchanged: no key bar, no dialog, no manifest or CSP line); real iPhone: the user's screenshot shows the key bar directly above iOS's form-assist bar in the standalone app.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the iOS software keyboard and Safari chrome. Covered by KeyBar.test.tsx, ctrlMap.test.ts, WebShareSessionView.keybar.test.tsx, style.keybar.test.ts, style.mobile.test.ts, visualViewportInset.test.ts and mobile-iphone.spec.ts (Section 4 MOB-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 14-10, 14-11, 14-12 and 14-13 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 14 SC1.
- **M-147** A pinch changes the terminal font, not the page, and sends one claim per pinch; a relaunch returns to the host's font (SC1, MOB-01).
  1. In the iPhone web view put two fingers on the terminal text and pinch out, then in. Watch whether the page zooms. Close and reopen the page.
  2. PASS when: the text size changes and the page does not zoom; each pinch makes exactly one grid resize on the host; after reopening the font is the host's again.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22. Simulator S-03 PASS: pinch out gave one resize (columns 48 to 20, Esc width unchanged); pinch in with the fingers on the terminal gave one resize (columns 20 to 28); a whole-view pinch in opened Safari's tab overview in the simulator, which the rig cannot tell from a real thumb and finger. Not run on the real iPhone (pinch not reported). The relaunch-returns-to-the-host-font half is pinned only by the in-memory font tests, not run on a device.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real two-finger gesture on iOS. Covered by pinchZoom.test.ts, TerminalPanel.pinch.test.tsx and WebShareSessionView.font.test.tsx (Section 4 MOB-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 14-11 and 14-13 SUMMARYs; ROADMAP Phase 14 SC1.
- **M-148** The view resumes by itself after sleep and after a Wi-Fi/cellular switch on a real iPhone with the Tailscale app, with no missing or duplicated output (SC3, MOB-03).
  1. With the Portty icon open on a shared session that prints a counter once a second, lock the phone for 10 minutes and unlock it. Turn Wi-Fi off (cellular, Tailscale still connected), wait about 30 s, turn it on. Switch to another app for 2 minutes and back. Scroll back over each gap.
  2. PASS when: each time the view resumes by itself (no Reload tap), the counter continues and the numbers are consecutive across the gap with nothing missing and nothing repeated.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, real iPhone, PASS (user, PHONE-2): "All three worked, no gaps": the 10-minute lock, Wi-Fi off and on, and the 2-minute app switch. Seconds to resume not reported; iOS version not reported.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real suspension, cellular and roaming, and the Tailscale app's VPN (WebKit bug 308073 shows only on a device). Covered by relayClient.test.ts, TerminalPanel.reconnect.test.tsx and SessionEndedBanner.test.tsx (Section 4 MOB-03 rows).
  - _Source:_ 14-04, 14-08 and 14-13 SUMMARYs; 14-SECURITY-REVIEW.md (fidelity caveat); ROADMAP Phase 14 SC3.
- **M-149** The same-account gate on real machines: a same-user Mac opens the link and still lists the host, another account is refused everywhere, a host tailscaled fault gives the retry page, and the WhoIs cost is small (SC5, MOB-05).
  1. Office on HEAD with a shared session, its link in a 0600 file on the home VM. From the home VM request the app page, the info route, a link page, the discovery probe and a WebSocket upgrade with `curl` (headers from files, nothing printed); open the link in home Safari. Install the `vmwhois` build on the office and repeat (another account). Write its `fault` mode and reload; then write `pass`. On HEAD run the `p14dev -whois-bench` helper in the office VM and time a cold `/app/` load.
  2. PASS when: the same account gets 200 on the app and info routes, 302 on the link page, 401 with `X-Portty: 1` on the probe and 101 on the WebSocket, and the home sidebar lists the office; another account gets 403 with `X-Portty: 1` on every gated route with the refusal page body equal to web/user-refused.html, no join in the host log, and the open routes (`/dashboard`, `/assets/`) still 200; the fault gives 503 with no marker and the retry page, the home sidebar drops the host's group and returns after the fault ends, and Try again recovers; the host's own address resolves to itself with the same user; the cold-load estimate stays under 300 ms and each p95 under 50 ms, else discuss a verdict cache.
  - Accepted limits (user decisions 2026-11-22): UI-A, accepted: the other account is shown with the `vmwhois` build, which rewrites the real WhoIs answer, and tests pin tagged and shared-in nodes; UI-B, keep uncached (the estimate 82.8 ms per cold load against the rule's 300 ms). The P-03 load of 64 s after Try again was a VM Safari effect (the real iPhone loaded in under 5 s), kept as a residual note.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, HEAD and the `vmwhois` build: P-01, P-02, P-03 and P-04 PASS (peer status p95 6.88 ms, WhoIs p95 4.95 ms, 7 gated requests per cold load; the sidebar group dropped 21 to 26 s after the fault and returned 31 s after it ended). Evidence in .planning/phases/14-mobile-web-app/14-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real tailscaled, a second Mac and real Safari. Covered by whois_gate_test.go, whois_test.go and web-same-user.spec.ts (Section 4 MOB-05 rows).
  - _Source:_ 14-01, 14-03, 14-06 and 14-12 SUMMARYs; 14-SECURITY-REVIEW.md; ROADMAP Phase 14 SC5.
- **M-150** A lone Esc acts at once in vim and in a raw key reader, in a local tab, a peer tab and the browser share view (SC1, MOB-01).
  1. In the office session run vim, press `i`, type text, press Esc alone and watch the mode line; run a raw key reader (`stty raw -echo` and a loop logging each byte with its time), press Esc alone, wait 3 s, press `x`. Do it in the office local tab, in a home peer tab and in home Safari's browser share view.
  2. PASS when: the Esc byte `1b` is logged within a fraction of a second of the press and about 3 s before the `x` (not together with it), and vim leaves Insert mode without another key (plain vim waits about 1 s for a possible escape sequence on its own).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22. Before the change E-00 (local tab) and E-01 (peer tab) PASS as the hold: `1b` arrived with the next key. After: P-05 PASS on HEAD (local tab 0.075 s after the press and 3.145 s before `x`; peer tab 0.14 s and 3.18 s; Safari's browser view `1b` 3.05 s before `x`; vim left Insert 1.116 s after the Esc, 0.211 s with its own wait at 50 ms). Home Safari needed a real Escape key code. Evidence in .planning/phases/14-mobile-web-app/14-VM-CHECKS.md.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real PTY, vim and three live clients. Covered by `TestInputAbsorber_LoneEscEndsFrame`, `TestInputAbsorber_EscBracketSplitLeaks` and `TestRelay_LoneEscReachesPTY` (oscabsorb_test.go, oscabsorb_relay_test.go; Section 4 MOB-01 rows).
  - _Source:_ 14-02 and 14-12 SUMMARYs; 14-SECURITY-REVIEW.md (Lone Esc); ROADMAP Phase 14 SC1.
- **M-151** The launcher page's steps and link format match the real Share flow (SC4, MOB-04).
  1. In the office GUI open an unshared session, click Share in the toolbar, turn on Share this session, read the warning, click Copy, and compare the copied link's shape and the warning sentence with docs/launcher-app.md.
  2. PASS when: the names (Share, Share this session, Copy) and the link form match the launcher text, the warning reads "Treat this link like a password. It only opens on devices signed in to your Tailscale account.", and the launcher has no input, form, script, token or socket.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-22, HEAD: P-07 PASS (the Copy path; AirDrop and Universal Clipboard cannot run in the VMs, so the launcher's step 2 was not exercised in the VMs; on the real iPhone the link did reach the phone, by a method the user did not report). Evidence in .planning/phases/14-mobile-web-app/14-VM-CHECKS.md.
