### Category BF — v1.2 Hardening (Phase 15)

Rig: the Tart VMs (office `portty-test`, home `portty-test-2`, both ephemeral tailnet nodes: never stop them for hours, never log them out, never `tailscale down`; drop traffic with pf), ad-hoc or notarized builds of the phase's final commit installed with `rm -rf` and `ditto`, and for M-153 a real terminal emulator. M-155 uses only the office VM, builds made on the host and copied with `tar` over SSH, and no tailnet. Nothing here runs on the user's Mac against its live tailscaled or a 100.x address, and real daemons from two builds never run on the host. All four items are recorded by 15-09 and run at /gsd-verify-work.

- **M-152** Clicking "Portty X is available" brings Portty forward with the update banner and starts no download (SC5).
  1. Rig: Tart VM `portty-test`, a notarized same-team build and a newer release on the fake feed (the M-92 setup). Hide the window (Quit GUI Only), let the update notification post, click it. Dismiss the banner, quit and relaunch, then click the notification left in Notification Center.
  2. PASS when: the window comes forward, `Portty X is available.` and `Download` show, no `Downloading…` progress appears until Download is pressed, and the click after relaunch also works.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-23, vmpreview build of 7ca43451 signed locally with the Developer ID (not notarized), office VM, fakegh newer 9.9.9: PASS. Click in Notification Center brought the window forward with `Portty 9.9.9 is available.` and Download, no download until Download was pressed; a notification from the previous process clicked after Quit Everything and relaunch also worked; a different notification id left the banner dismissed. Rig notes: VM notifications for app.portty.Portty were Off from an old denial (turned On/Persistent); clicking a notification removes it, so the relaunch step re-posts by deleting update_notified first.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real UserNotifications delegate on a notarized bundle and the system Notification Center. Covered in process by `TestNotificationClick_ShowsWindowAndBanner`, `TestNotificationClick_IgnoresOtherIDs` (app_update_test.go) and App.update.test.tsx (`update:show`).
  - _Source:_ 15-06-PLAN.md and 15-06-SUMMARY.md; ROADMAP Phase 15 SC5; todo 2026-11-18-update-notification-click.
- **M-153** Full-screen apps stay off the `portty attach` bar (SC1).
  1. Rig: a real terminal emulator attaching to a session (local, then a remote host:session from the home VM). Run vim, then Claude Code; resize the terminal; open the same session in a GUI tab sized taller than the CLI terminal. Passive-watch step: with the GUI tab owning the grid (it claimed last, nothing typed in the CLI), start vim from the GUI tab while the CLI only watches, and record what the CLI shows. Then type in the CLI, and separately resize the CLI terminal. Quit vim and run `tput lines` in the CLI.
  2. PASS when: nothing draws on the bottom bar row once the CLI has typed or resized, `tput lines` is the terminal's rows minus 1 after typing in the CLI, and the bar text stays intact. The passive-watch observation is recorded, not a PASS condition: under the grid-owner model (CONTEXT [Attach bar], INPT-06) a CLI that only watches a taller GUI-owned grid shows the app's layout for that grid, which can cover the bar row until the CLI types or resizes; that is pre-existing behaviour, not a regression from 15-01.
  - Accepted limit (user decision 2026-11-22): with `--status-top`, an app that addresses absolute rows (vim) can paint the top row, because the bar does not use origin mode.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-23, build v1.2.0-vm16 (7ca43451), Terminal.app in the VMs, local (office) and remote (home→office): PASS. Bar row intact after every CLI type/resize (grid log `why=input`/`why=claim`), `tput lines` = rows-1 (24 of 25 local, 22 of 23 remote). `less` stood in for Claude Code (not installed in the VMs). Passive watch: CLI shows the GUI grid's layout scrolled, bar row intact. --status-top: vim paints row 1, bar repaints within 1 s (accepted limit).
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real terminal emulator and full-screen apps. Covered in process by `TestBarRows`, `TestWatchResize_ClaimsBarRows` (internal/attach/attach_test.go), `TestBar_ReservedRows`, `TestInitialResizeFrame` and `TestHub_GridAttachBarRows`.
  - _Source:_ 15-01-PLAN.md and 15-01-SUMMARY.md; ROADMAP Phase 15 SC1; todo 2026-11-21-attach-statusbar-overwritten.
- **M-154** An ended peer tab stays "Session ended" and a reachable host never shows "Can't reach this Mac" after a kill (SC2).
  1. Rig: the two Tart VMs on the real tailnet (office host, home viewer). Share one session on the office VM and open it in a home peer tab. End it on the office (exit), switch to another home tab and back to the ended tab. Sample the home sidebar every 5 s for 40 s. Then share a second office session (open it on the home VM so the office row is listed), drop the office's share-port traffic with pf (never `tailscale down`) and sample again; with no share left the office leaves the list before pf can change its row, so the second share is required (amended 2026-11-23 after the first run).
  2. PASS when: the ended tab shows `Session ended — the owner stopped this session.` (never `<host> stopped sharing this session.`); after the last share ends the office row shows "Nothing shared" until it drops out of the list, never `Can't reach this Mac. Check that it's awake and on your tailnet.`; with pf dropping, the can't-reach line shows.
  - Sidebar branch: 15-07-SUMMARY.md reads `Sidebar branch: fixed`, so the "Nothing shared" clause stands and SC2 is covered by 15-05 (tab) and 15-07 (sidebar).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-23, build v1.2.0-vm15 (1c8d3a09): FAIL. Sidebar clause PASS ("Nothing shared" then drops out, 0 can't-reach in 2 runs); ended-tab clause FAIL twice: after switching away and back the banner read `portty-vm-office stopped sharing this session.` because the ended session was the host's last share, the host stopped its share server, and the redial returned `not_sharing`; pf clause not meaningful as then written. Fix: `peerConnectVerdict` maps `not_sharing` to ended after the session-ended close (7ca43451). Rerun 2026-11-23 on v1.2.0-vm16: PASS. Ended tab stayed `Session ended — the owner stopped this session.` after switch-back in 2/2 runs (redial refused); sidebar `Nothing shared` then drops out, 0 can't-reach in 2/2; with a second share live, pf drop showed `Can't reach this Mac. Check that it's awake and on your tailnet.` 16 s after the drop and the tab recovered after pf was lifted.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real host kill over the real tailnet (a TCP reset from a closed share port versus a timeout). Covered in process by `TestMetaStatus_TriState` and `TestFetchAllPeerSessionsMeta_RefusedIsReachableEmpty` (internal/tailnet/tailnet_test.go) and the ended-verdict cases in App.open-remote.test.tsx, closeReasons.test.ts and TerminalPanel.guestClose.test.tsx.
  - _Source:_ 15-05-PLAN.md and 15-07-PLAN.md; ROADMAP Phase 15 SC2; todo 2026-11-21-ended-peer-tab-banner.
- **M-155** Upgrades keep working across real processes with the Phase 15 handoff checks (SC4; 15-03 post-COMMIT tighten, 15-08 O-6 pid check).
  1. Rig: the office Tart VM `portty-test`, no tailnet use, temp HOMEs under `/tmp`. On the host build, from the phase's final commit and exactly as M-114 step 1, A (`v1.2.0-itA`), B (`v1.2.0-itB`), B-fault (`-tags vmpreview`), the v1.1 build and the `go test -c ./internal/daemon/` binary, plus P, the pre-phase product commit `09c6c07a`, built from a temporary `git worktree` with `-X main.Version=v1.2.0-it0` (remove the worktree afterwards). Copy all with `tar` over SSH. Nothing from two builds runs on the host.
  2. In the VM run TestHandoffIT as M-114 step 2.
  3. Chain: `mktemp -d /tmp/pch-XXXXXX` as a short HOME, start `P daemon` in the background with only HOME changed (the M-114 rig's env: no `PORTTY_*` variables), create two sessions with `P new`, record the socket mode with `stat -f %Lp` on `$HOME/Library/Application Support/portty/daemon.sock` and the ids from `P list --local`.
  4. Run `A list --local` and record its exit code and stderr, the socket mode, `curl -s --unix-socket <socket> http://localhost/health` and the ids.
  5. Run `B list --local` and record the same.
  6. Stop the daemon with `curl -s --unix-socket <socket> -X POST http://localhost/shutdown`, search every `daemon.log*` in that HOME for `does not match the socket peer`, then remove the HOME.
  - PASS when: TestHandoffIT `upgrade`, `fault` and `from-v1.1` all pass with no `portty-it` process or `/tmp/pit-*` dir left (upgrade: B's pid check accepted A across real processes; fault: the takeover killed at new:before-ready left A serving); in the chain the socket is 755 under P, 600 after the first upgrade and still 600 after the second; both `list --local` runs exit 0 with no `couldn't move` line; `/health` reports `v1.2.0-itA` then `v1.2.0-itB`; the same two session ids after each step; and no daemon log has `does not match the socket peer`.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-23, builds from aba14c53 (product) and 1c8d3a09 (test binary), office VM: PASS. TestHandoffIT upgrade/fault/from-v1.1 PASS (first run stopped at a stale `API != 2` assertion, fixed in 1c8d3a09); upgrade accepted B's pid check across real processes, same 3 ids, shells ppid 1, A exit 0, viewer no gap. Chain: socket 755 → 600 → 600, both `list --local` rc 0, /health it0 → itA → itB, same 2 ids, 0 `does not match the socket peer`.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real daemons from two builds must not run on the user's Mac and the VM has no Go toolchain (M-114); in process the checks are `TestHandoff_RefusesPIDMismatch`, `TestHandoff_TightensSocketMode` and `TestHandoff_HappyPath`, and the dialing-side `LOCAL_PEERPID` result comes from one research probe (15-RESEARCH Item D).
  - _Source:_ 15-03-PLAN.md, 15-08-PLAN.md; plan check 2026-11-22.

### Category BG — v1.2 Cleanup (Phase 16)

Rig: the Tart VM `portty-test` (never stopped for hours, never logged out, never `tailscale down`), a build of the phase's final commit installed with `rm -rf` and `ditto`. Nothing here runs on the user's Mac, and no tailnet is needed. Recorded by 16-04 and runs at /gsd-verify-work.

- **M-156** Every launch path still opens Portty, and CLI flags never start the GUI (SC1, SC2, D-01).
  1. Launch from Finder (double-click), quit the window, launch from the Dock icon, then `open -a Portty`, `open -a Portty --args -NSDocumentRevisionsDebugMode YES` and `open -a Portty --args -ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES`.
  2. Over SSH run `/Applications/Portty.app/Contents/MacOS/Portty --version`, then `-v`, then `--bogus`, with `echo $?` after each and `pgrep -x Portty` before and after.
  3. Create two sessions, rename one with `portty rename NAME NEWNAME`, kill one by name with `portty kill NAME`, then create two sessions with the same name and try `portty kill` with that shared name, then try `portty kill` with the first 4 characters of a remaining session's ID.
  4. PASS when: every launch in step 1 shows the window; `--version` and `-v` print the version and exit 0, `--bogus` prints `portty: unknown flag "--bogus"` and the usage and exits 2, and no new Portty process appears for any of them; rename and kill by name act on the named session (`portty list --local`), and the duplicate name is refused with the `more than one session is named` line and both sessions remain; the 4-character ID prefix is refused with `no session named` and the session remains (kill and rename need at least 8 characters for an ID prefix).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-23, **PASS** in `portty-test` (macOS 27.2), build `v1.2.0-vm17` from 57cb9ca2 installed with `rm -rf` and `ditto`. Step 1: Finder double-click, Dock reopen after closing the window, cold launch from a pinned Dock icon, `open -a Portty` and both `--args` forms (GUI argv carried `-NSDocumentRevisionsDebugMode YES` / `-ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES`) each showed `AXWindow Title=Portty`. Step 2: `--version` and `-v` printed `v1.2.0-vm17` rc 0, `--bogus` printed the unknown-flag line and usage rc 2, `pgrep -x Portty` 0 before and after each. Step 3: duplicate `shell` refused with the `more than one session is named` line (both remained); rename by full ID and by name worked; a 4-character ID prefix was refused by rename and kill with `no session named`; kill by name and by an 8-character prefix worked. VM Dock and test app restored afterwards.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real LaunchServices launches of a signed bundle, which must not happen on the user's Mac; covered in process by `TestLaunchMode` (dispatch_test.go), `TestCmdKill_ByName` and `TestCmdRename_ByName` (cmd_cli_test.go) and 16-01's built-binary run.
  - _Source:_ 16-01-PLAN.md and 16-01-SUMMARY.md; 16-CONTEXT D-01; todos 2026-11-23-version-flag-hangs and 2026-11-23-kill-by-name-fails.

### Category BH — v2.0 Desktop Feasibility Spike (Phase 17)

Rig: the user's office Mac (the shared Mac, macOS 27.2.x) and personal MacBook Air (the viewer), both on the user's tailnet. Probes are rebuilt from `spike/desktop` with `bash spike/desktop/bundle/build.sh` (Developer ID identity, never ad-hoc; `--shell` builds the throwaway Wails viewer shell). Nothing here runs without the owner's go on that Mac, and only the spike's own test chart is captured on a personal Mac. Afterwards revoke with `tccutil reset ScreenCapture app.portty.spike`, `tccutil reset Accessibility app.portty.spike` and `tccutil reset PostEvent app.portty.spike`, `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/app.portty.spike.daemon` and remove its plist. Never commit tokens, tailnet addresses or real desktop frames. Results feed `.planning/phases/17-desktop-feasibility-spike/17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md`; each item names its row. Recorded by 17-12; the real-Mac items run when Phase 19 is verified, M-160 after a real 30-day wait.

- **M-157** The office Mac and the home Mac reach each other on a direct tailnet path, and the desktop stream uses it with no STUN, TURN or cloud server (DESK-02, SC1).
  1. On the office Mac start the spike in the daemon shape (path B LaunchAgent with `serve --source sck` bound to its tailnet address and a fresh token).
  2. On the home Mac run `tailscale ping -c 5 <office Mac>` before and after a 15 s Safari viewer session (`ice=lite`), and keep a packet capture on the office Mac for UDP ports 3478, 5353 and 19302 during the session.
  3. PASS when: both pings read direct (no "via DERP"), frames decode at the captured size, the selected pair is on tailnet addresses, `lsof` shows only the tailnet address, and no spike packet goes to 3478, 5353 or 19302.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the two real Macs on the user's tailnet; the test VMs always connect via DERP (test-rig limit, accepted at the 17-12 closure checkpoint).
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R01 (R01-vm, R01-host)
  - _From Phase 19:_ run it with Portty's always-available desktop instead of the spike: the link's media rides the direct path (no STUN, TURN or cloud server, DESK-02), checked with `lsof` and the packet capture as above; M-173 step 3 settles the first-frame time on it.

- **M-158** A daemon started from an SSH shell gets no Accessibility of its own when the SSH daemon wrapper holds none (DSHR-01; documents why the product never starts the daemon from SSH).
  1. At the office Mac's keyboard (not over SSH), turn off Accessibility for sshd-keygen-wrapper in System Settings, with the spike daemon's own Accessibility grant on.
  2. From an SSH shell start `Spike.app/Contents/MacOS/spike probe-loop --ax-move` with nohup, let it run 30 s, then turn sshd-keygen-wrapper's Accessibility back to its earlier state at the keyboard.
  3. PASS when: the SSH-started process reads ax_trusted false (or posts no event) and tccd names sshd-keygen-wrapper, not app.portty.spike, while a launchd-started daemon (`launchctl kickstart`) posts with the app's own grant.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the case needs the SSH wrapper's grant off, which removes the SSH tools that would turn it back on; only a person at the Mac can run it (accepted at the 17-12 closure checkpoint).
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R08 (R08-D)

- **M-159** The office Mac's display idle and system sleep policy, and what a viewer sees across display sleep and wake there (DPRM-04).
  1. On the office Mac record `pmset -g` and `pmset -g assertions` on battery and on power.
  2. With a viewer connected, let the display sleep (`pmset displaysleepnow`), wait 20 s, wake from the keyboard and unlock.
  3. PASS when: the record shows whether the office Mac locks and enters system sleep after display sleep (as the MacBook Air did 5.5 s later) and the time to the first frame after the wake with the daemon's display-awake and system-sleep assertions held, or the stream restart after SCError -3815.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ real display and power hardware of the office Mac; R18 ran only on the personal MacBook Air.
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R18 (R18-host)
  - _From Phase 19:_ run it with Portty's always-available desktop instead of the spike: the product holds the display and system-sleep assertions for a viewer and the AC-only assertion (DPRM-04); M-174 is the battery version of this item.

- **M-160** The real 30-day Screen Recording re-approval prompt on the office Mac, and what capture does while it waits (DPRM-03).
  1. Note the date the office Mac's capture host was first approved (the approval store's LastAlerted).
  2. After 30 days, with the daemon capturing and a viewer connected, watch the office Mac's screen and the stream.
  3. PASS when: the prompt's wording and buttons are recorded, frames are counted before, during and after the prompt, and the detection DPRM-03 uses (store read or nudge plus frame check) reports "waiting for approval at the Mac".
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real 30-day wait on the real Mac; the spike backdated the store in a disposable clone, which raised the bypass-picker prompt but not the real monthly one.
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R19
  - _From Phase 19:_ run it with Portty's always-available desktop instead of the spike: the product's lapse detection (DPRM-03) must show Waiting for approval at the Mac in Settings, the tray and the viewer, and the lapse notification is M-176.

- **M-161** The office Mac's hardware H.264 encoder at its native display size and 30 fps: CPU, latency and sharpness (DESK-01, DESK-07).
  1. On the office Mac run the spike's host-run with the test chart moving for 95 s and a viewer joined.
  2. Read the timeline's encoder readback, CPU, encode latency and the viewer's decode and drop counts.
  3. PASS when: the encoder reads back hardware, frames hold near 30 fps at the native size with no drops in the motion window, and the numbers are recorded next to R20-host's.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the office Mac's real encoder; R20 ran only on the personal MacBook Air.
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R20 (R20-host)
  - _From Phase 19:_ run it with Portty's always-available desktop instead of the spike: read the capture size (DESK-01, at most 2560 on the long side) and the viewer's frame width in the `#debug` read-out; M-173 checks the sharpness.

- **M-162** System audio capture from the Mac's real output device reaches the viewer (DCTL-07).
  1. On a real Mac play a known sound through the built-in speakers or headphones while `opusprobe --source sck` runs 10 s, then a 5 s run in silence.
  2. Read buffer counts, peak sample and Opus packet sizes.
  3. PASS when: the sound run gives buffers with a non-zero peak and full-size packets, and the silent run gives peak 0 and silence packets.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the VM has only a virtual output device; the real device needs a real Mac.
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R21

- **M-163** On the office Mac the in-daemon capture host is attributed and listed as in the VMs, and the grant is confirmed by a fresh-process probe (`portty daemon perm-probe`) or by effect (Test Capture), not by preflight polling in a long-running process (FA-1; DPRM-01).
  1. On the office Mac, after a clean reset, bootstrap the spike daemon's LaunchAgent and let it request Screen Recording and Accessibility.
  2. Answer each dialog, then read the grant every 10 s for 3 minutes with a fresh-process probe (`portty daemon perm-probe`, a new process each time) or by effect (the setup sheet's Test Capture); a long-running process's own check is cached and is not evidence. If it stays false, add the grant by hand in System Settings and read again.
  3. PASS when: tccd names app.portty.spike as the accessing identity for ScreenCapture, Accessibility and PostEvent, System Settings lists Spike under Screen and System Audio Recording and Accessibility and the LaunchAgent under Login Items (signing developer's name), and the record says which grant path made the fresh probe or Test Capture read true.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ real TCC prompts on the office Mac; every attribution row (R02 to R09) ran only in a VM, and in host session 1 an Allow click did not make preflight read true.
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R04 (FA-1 in `## FEAS-02 decision`; R09-B)

- **M-164** The office Mac's Tailscale mode: network extension or userspace networking (milestone research gap).
  1. On the office Mac read how Tailscale runs (the app's network extension or a userspace `tailscaled`), and whether a `utun` interface carries the Mac's tailnet address.
  2. Start the spike's serve bound to that address and join from the home Mac.
  3. PASS when: the mode is recorded, a network-extension Mac binds the tailnet address and serves the viewer, and for userspace mode the record notes that a kernel-bound socket cannot reach the tailnet (Phase 19 must detect it and refuse with a clear message).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the office Mac's own Tailscale install, which no test rig reproduces.
  - _Source:_ 17-SPIKE-MATRIX.md row R01 (M-164 in `## FEAS-02 decision`)
  - _From Phase 19:_ run it with Portty's always-available desktop instead of the spike: in userspace-networking mode the Settings switch is refused with the userspace sentence and the daemon does not start the desktop (`TestDesktopAPI_PollerIgnoresOffAndUserspace`, `TestDesktopAPI_UserspaceRefusalClearsWhileOff` and the Settings test cover the logic).

### Category BI — Self-Installing Updates (UPD-05..UPD-09)

Rig: two ad-hoc rig builds of one commit, 1.8.0 (N) and 1.8.1 (N+1), made with `.planning/phases/18-self-installing-updates/rig/p18-rig.sh build` (`-tags "wailsassets vmpreview"`, `-ldflags "-X main.Version=vX.Y.Z"`, `wails.json` productVersion patched per build so the two Info.plists differ, restored after; `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11`). The `vmpreview` tag points the update check at fakegh (`cmd/fakegh`) on `127.0.0.1:8788` inside the VM. It reads `$HOME/p18/verify-mode` (`adhoc`: fakes only the codesign and spctl answers an ad-hoc build cannot pass; every other tool runs for real) and `$HOME/p18/fault` (VM-only faults: `exit-before-daemon`, `exit-after-daemon`, `hang-before-quit`). It is never used in a release build (`tests/check-brand.sh` keeps it out of scripts/, build.sh and .github). Everything runs in Tart VM `portty-test` (macOS 27.2) through `vm.sh`: `deploy-n`, `dmg`, `feed`, `state ROW`, `quit-gui`, `clean`. Sessions come from the installed CLI and the expect driver, the GUI is read with `axdump`, and dialog buttons are pressed with an AXPress helper. Evidence is in `.planning/phases/18-self-installing-updates/18-VM-CHECKS.md`, with the rig captures `evidence/ROW.state` (header `p18-state row=ROW`) and per-row timelines and logs under `evidence/`. VM limits to keep in mind: Gatekeeper assessments are disabled in the guest (`spctl --status`: `assessments disabled`), so an spctl "accepted" there is not an enforcement result. SIP refuses `chflags` and `chmod` on `/Applications`. On macOS 27.2 `hdiutil attach -mountpoint` and `hdiutil detach` print deprecation warnings (use `diskutil image attach` / `diskutil eject`) but still work; `updater.Mount` uses that same command (deferred finding).

- **M-165** VM update path: one click on the banner's Update installs N+1 with sessions alive (UPD-05, UPD-06; Portty 18-01, 18-03, 18-05, 18-07, 18-08, 18-10; Claude runs it in portty-test).
  1. `deploy-n` N, start fakegh offering N+1, launch, make two sessions (a 1 s shell ticker after 300 `HIST-s1-n` lines, and a vim timer), share the ticker session (loopback link kept in a 0600 file, never printed).
  2. Start the 0.4 s process poller, the 1 s window lister and the axdump banner poller; click Update once; run `state ROW-before` and `state ROW`.
  3. Repeat the plain update 6 times from a fresh N (A9-fix) and once with a Developer ID N (`p18-rig.sh sign`, `verify-mode` removed, feed serving the real published v1.2.0 DMG) for P2d-bundle and R3. For F6, SIGKILL the installer after the swap and before health, then open Portty by hand.
  4. PASS when: exactly one GUI at N+1 from `/Applications`, daemon `/health` at `vN+1`, the same session ids and shell pids, a fresh viewer shows the full history with gapless ticks, the share link answers 302/200 before and after and 401 for a wrong token, the old bundle is in the Trash, the cache DMG is gone (`cache_dmgs=0`), no staging or run dir is left, no dialog appears, and `update.log` holds no path. The installer runs from the whole-bundle copy in `update/run-*` (P2d-bundle; a lone executable copy is killed by AMFI under a Team ID, P2d). Every relaunch ends with a GUI (A9-fix: `installer: open attempt 1 failed` followed by a successful retry is fine). After F6, the next launch takes the sessions over and the sweep trashes the leftovers once they are older than 1 h.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-26, build `e03f4ef1`: R1 PASS (history 300/300, ticks gapless, share 302/200/401 before and after, click to daemon `v1.8.1` 4.95 s), R3 PASS (Developer ID 1.1.90 to the real v1.2.0, un-granted, no AMFI kill, no dialog), F6 PASS. Earlier builds: P2d-bundle CONFIRMED 2026-11-25 on `d132e572`; A9-fix PASS 2026-11-26 on `b968a6ef` (run 5 hit the race and the retry recovered it); R1-lite PASS 2026-11-25 (18-08 tree on `32b69fc3`). Evidence: 18-VM-CHECKS.md rows R1-lite, P2d-bundle, A9-fix, R1, R3, F6.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real LaunchServices relaunch, AMFI, App Management, the Trash and a live daemon takeover need a GUI session on real macOS. The logic is covered by the Section 4 UPD-05 and UPD-06 rows.
  - _Source:_ 18-CONTEXT.md [Installer], 18-08-PLAN.md, 18-07-PLAN.md, 18-10-PLAN.md.

- **M-166** VM rollback: a broken N+1 puts N back by itself (UPD-07; Portty 18-03, 18-07; Claude runs it in portty-test).
  1. As M-165 steps 1-2, with `~/p18/fault` written before the click: R2-A `exit-before-daemon vN+1`, R2-B `exit-after-daemon vN+1`, R2-D `hang-before-quit vN`. For R2-C, set `chflags uchg /Applications/Portty.app` after the banner and before the click, and clear it after.
  2. Write the deadline W before the click (click-to-new-GUI + HealthTimeout 60 s + StopWait 5 s + KillWait 2 s + 30 s).
  3. PASS when: R2-A and R2-B roll back (`installer: rolled-back gui-exited`) within W with N installed and one GUI. The sessions keep their shell pids, in R2-B through the committed N+1 daemon with no DEBT-08 restart dialog. The banner shows `Portty N+1 didn't start, so Portty put version N back. The download is shown in Finder.` with the kept DMG revealed and `skip-version` set, and the automatic check does not offer N+1 again. R2-C ends `failed swap` with N relaunched and `Portty couldn't install version N+1. The download is shown in Finder.` R2-D kills only the old GUI after QuitWait (20 s) and the update completes.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-26: R2-A PASS (click-to-rollback 3.52 s, W=100.3 s) and R2-B PASS (3.23 s) on `e03f4ef1`; R2-C and R2-D PASS on `b968a6ef`. Evidence: 18-VM-CHECKS.md rows R2-A to R2-D.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real GUI that exits or hangs, LaunchServices and the daemon takeover in a GUI session; the logic is covered by the Section 4 UPD-07 rows.
  - _Source:_ 18-03-PLAN.md, 18-07-PLAN.md.

- **M-167** VM first launch from a DMG: the mover installs Portty and leaves one copy running (UPD-08; Portty 18-06, 18-09; Claude runs it in portty-test).
  1. Make the N+1 DMG in the VM (`p18-rig.sh dmg`), copy it to the VM's `~/Downloads` as `portty-vX.Y.Z-darwin-arm64.dmg`, open it like Finder (`open` on the file), then `open /Volumes/Portty/Portty.app`. Empty the Trash and `~/Downloads` of Portty items before each row, and reset the Downloads consent service-wide (`tccutil reset SystemPolicyDownloadsFolder`) before a prompt run.
  2. Rows: R4-a no installed copy; R4-b N installed and running with two sessions, Replace; R4-c the same, Cancel; R4-d N+1 installed; R4-e `/Applications` not writable (SIP refuses chmod in the guest, so the opened app is given an access() interposer through `open --env DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES=...`); R4-f a browser-style quarantine flag on the DMG; R4-g a second run from the same DMG after R4-a.
  3. After each row read the menu-bar item count (AXExtrasMenuBar of every `app.portty.Portty` process) and run `state ROW`.
  4. PASS when: R4-a installs to `/Applications`, relaunches from there, detaches the volume and moves the DMG to the Trash, leaving no staging dir. The only dialog is macOS's one-time Downloads prompt, which comes after the result is written and is accepted (user decision 2026-11-25; Don't Allow keeps the DMG and the install still succeeds, R4-smoke-deny-a2). R4-b shows `Replace Portty N in Applications?` / `This copy is version N+1.` in front of every window, then installs N+1 with the same shell pids and puts N and the DMG in the Trash. R4-c opens the installed N, stages nothing and leaves the DMG mounted and in `~/Downloads`. R4-d and R4-g open the installed copy unchanged (version, mtime), detach the volume and trash the DMG. R4-e installs to `~/Applications/Portty.app`. R4-f is not translocated, shows only the system's first-launch confirmation, and leaves the installed copy with no quarantine flag and no dialog on relaunch. Every row ends with exactly one GUI and one menu-bar item.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-26, build `e03f4ef1`: R4-a to R4-g PASS; R4-e-sandbox OBSERVED (a sandbox-exec mechanism stops the installer from running `/bin/ps`, a rig artifact); R4-f-gatekeeper BLOCKED (assessments disabled in the guest; the real Gatekeeper path is M-169 part 1). R4-smoke PASS 2026-11-26 on `79be910c`. Evidence: 18-VM-CHECKS.md rows R4-smoke, R4-smoke-deny-a2, R4-a to R4-g.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a mounted disk image, Finder's open, LaunchServices, the system's first-launch and Downloads dialogs and a real Trash need a GUI session on real macOS; the logic is covered by the Section 4 UPD-08 rows.
  - _Source:_ 18-CONTEXT.md [Mover] and the Downloads-prompt decision (option A with refinement A2), 18-06-PLAN.md, 18-09-PLAN.md.

- **M-168** VM install failures reveal the download with a clear sentence and keep Portty running (UPD-09; Portty 18-02, 18-07; Claude runs it in portty-test).
  1. From a fresh N with the banner up, force one failure per row before the click: F1 N running from a root-owned 755 folder; F2 N on a 40 MB APFS image with under twice the app's size free; F3 fakegh serving random bytes under the DMG name; F4 an N+1 whose bundle version does not match; F5 `chflags uchg` on the bundle (swap) and on `~/Applications` (staging); F7 a kqueue helper that makes the new staging folder read-only before `ditto` writes into it.
  2. PASS when: each row shows its own sentence on the warning banner: F1 `Portty doesn't have permission to replace itself from this account. The download is shown in Finder.`; F2 `There isn't enough free disk space to install the update. ...`; F3 `Portty couldn't open the download. ...`; F4 `The new version didn't pass the safety check, so it wasn't installed. ...`; F5 `Portty couldn't install version N+1. ...` (swap) and the permission sentence (staging); F7 `Portty couldn't copy the new version. ...`. Finder's `updates` window comes to the front with the DMG selected and its quarantine back on (`0081;...;Portty;`). The GUI stays up at N, nothing is swapped, and no staging dir or `mnt-*` is left (F5a's verified copy stays for the sweep).
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-26, build `e03f4ef1`: F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F7 PASS (side rows F5b-attempt1, F7-unreadable and F7-race-lost OBSERVED). Evidence: 18-VM-CHECKS.md rows F1 to F7.
  - _Residual risk:_ a genuine Developer-ID App Management denial is forced in the VM only by its errno (F5's `uchg`). It is seen for real only in P2d-bundle or R3, where macOS granted the same-Team exception and nothing was refused, and otherwise in M-169 part 1.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Finder's reveal, real disk-full and permission refusals and the native banner need a GUI session on real macOS; the logic is covered by the Section 4 UPD-09 rows.
  - _Source:_ 18-02-PLAN.md, 18-07-PLAN.md.

- **M-169** The office Mac, real releases (UPD-05..UPD-09; the owner runs it, the only office-Mac item in this category).
  - _Part 1, at the first self-installing release:_ on the office Mac with an installed v1.2.x running two ticking sessions (one with scrollback history) and a share link open from the home Mac over the tailnet, download the release DMG (`portty-vX.Y.Z-darwin-arm64.dmg`) in a browser, so it is quarantined and notarized. Open it in Finder and open Portty from the volume. Answer the Replace question with Replace. Accept the one-time Downloads prompt if it appears (user decision 2026-11-25).
  - _Part 2, at the release after that:_ with two ticking sessions and the share link open from the home Mac, click Update in the banner once.
  - PASS when: a dialog appears only when replacing (part 1: the one Replace question, plus at most the system's first-launch confirmation for the downloaded app and the one-time Downloads prompt). The sessions keep their shell pids and their history, and the share link opens from the home Mac over the tailnet before and after. The DMG is ejected and in the Trash (part 1) or deleted from the update cache (part 2), and the old app is in the Trash. No Gatekeeper or App Management dialog appears on the relaunch. If R3 or P2d-bundle had been BLOCKED, the App Management outcome would be recorded here (both ran: the kernel registered the same-Team AppBundles exception and nothing was refused).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - Phase 18 closes with M-169 human_needed: ROADMAP SC1's office-Mac half (Update clicked in the banner on the office Mac) stays unmet until part 2 runs; part 1 (the mover Replace path) is not counted as that proof.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs real signed and notarized releases on GitHub (two of them for part 2), the office Mac's real Gatekeeper, App Management and tailnet, and the owner's sessions. The VM has Gatekeeper assessments disabled and ad-hoc builds.
  - _Source:_ 18-09-PLAN.md flagged decisions; ROADMAP Phase 18 SC1; STATE blocker (self-install only helps upgrades from a build that has it).

- **M-170** The one-time Downloads-folder prompt on a real Mac, Developer ID release (UPD-08; Portty 18-06, 18-09; any real Mac, the home Mac is enough; not an office-Mac item).
  1. Move any installed Portty aside and reset the Downloads consent with `tccutil reset SystemPolicyDownloadsFolder`. The bundle-scoped form (`... app.portty.Portty`) fails with -10814 once no app has that bundle id.
  2. Download the release DMG in a browser to `~/Downloads`, open it in Finder and open Portty from the volume.
  3. PASS when: Portty is installed and running from `/Applications` before the prompt `“Portty” would like to access files in your Downloads folder.` appears. Allow puts the DMG in the Trash within a few seconds. On a repeat after a reset, Don't Allow keeps the DMG in `~/Downloads` and Portty stays installed and running. A later first install of a newer release DMG asks no second time (the consent belongs to the Developer ID app, while each ad-hoc rig build is asked again).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run on a real Mac. VM evidence (ad-hoc): R4-smoke and R4-a (Allow), R4-smoke-deny-a2 (Don't Allow), 2026-11-26.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real TCC prompt for a Developer ID app on a real Mac; the VM shows it only for ad-hoc rig builds.
  - _Source:_ user decision 2026-11-25 (18-CONTEXT.md, REQUIREMENTS UPD-08), refinement A2 (18-06-SUMMARY.md).

- **M-171** Finder's Put Back for the old Portty the installer moved to the Trash (UPD-05, UPD-06; Portty 18-05, 18-09; any real Mac; not an office-Mac item).
  1. After an update (banner Update or a mover Replace), open the Trash in Finder and select the old `Portty.app` (or `Portty HH-MM-SS-mmm.app`).
  2. Record whether File → Put Back is offered and, if used, where it restores the app. Then drag the old app out of the Trash into a scratch folder and open it.
  3. PASS when: the dragged-out copy opens as the older version (the documented recovery), and Put Back's state is recorded either way.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. Unchecked in the VM: driving Finder needs an Automation grant (`osascript` to Finder returned -1743 and no grant was made), and the installer's DesktopServices could not write the Trash's `.DS_Store` (`NSCocoaErrorDomain Code=513`, P3), which is where Finder keeps Put Back data. Put Back may therefore be greyed out.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Finder UI on a real Mac, and an Automation grant the rig does not make.
  - _Source:_ 18-VM-CHECKS.md row P3; 18-05-SUMMARY.md; 18-07-SUMMARY.md open finding 5.

### Category BI.1 — Apple Silicon Release Pipeline (REL-01, Phase 18.1)

Phase 18.1 sits between Phase 18 and Phase 19; the one item below is the first tag run of the arm64 release pipeline.

- **M-172** First tag run of the Apple Silicon release pipeline (REL-01; first run is Phase 23's v2.0.0-rc.1 draft; carries ROADMAP SC2's deferred runtime proof).
  - _Preconditions:_ the release environment's secrets exist; a user pushes the rc tag (Phase 23).
  1. The Release run passes "Release bundle is arm64-only with minos 14.0 (REL-01)", "Sign .app with hardened runtime" (`sign-bundle.sh` and `verify-bundle.sh --signed`), "Notarize .app" (`--notarized`), the three DMG steps for both names and the checksum step.
  2. The draft release holds both DMGs and a two-line `checksums.txt`, and its notes start with "Apple Silicon and macOS 14 or later only."
  3. Download both DMGs into a scratch directory (never `~/Downloads`), run `UPDATE_REAL_DMG=<dmg> GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11 go test -run TestRealDMG ./internal/updater` for each (not skipped), mount each read-only on a scratch mountpoint and check the app's `codesign -d -r-` designated line equals v1.2's, `lipo -archs` prints `arm64` and `vtool -show-build` shows minos 14.0, then detach.
  4. At the real v2.0 release, an installed v1.2.x copy finds, verifies and installs it through the universal name (with M-93).
  - PASS when all hold. SC2 runtime proof deferred to Phase 23 rc.1 (this item is its deferred runtime proof).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs a pushed tag, the release secrets, Apple's notary service and an installed older copy; local tests prove the gates with fixtures, a host Developer ID signature and an un-notarized two-name DMG rehearsal.
  - _Source:_ 18.1-02-PLAN.md, 18.1-03-PLAN.md.

### Category BJ — Unattended Desktop Viewing (Phase 19)

Rig: the user's office Mac and personal MacBook Air (the viewer) on the user's tailnet, plus the user's iPhone with Tailscale on; Portty installed from a Developer ID build or the first v2.0 release. Nothing here runs without the owner at the Mac, and no token, link or address is written down: links go from the office Mac to the other devices through a private channel and are deleted afterwards. The VM rows these items extend are in `.planning/phases/19-unattended-desktop-viewing/19-VM-CHECKS.md` (each item names its rows as _Source_); only what a VM cannot show is here. DSHR-01 as amended by the user on 2026-11-25 ("Start it anyway"): launchd primary; a fallback-started daemon is surfaced as degraded (Background service, Needs repair). M-176 is human_needed and stays visible here: the daemon-posted lapse notification with the GUI closed, on a notarized build. Phase 19 also turns M-157, M-159, M-160, M-161, M-163 and M-164 from spike checks into product checks (a line under each says how). Phase 20 numbers its items after M-179.

- **M-173** The office Mac's always-available desktop works end to end with the Portty window quit, viewed from the home Mac in Chrome and Safari, sharp on the Retina display (DESK-01, DESK-02, DESK-03, DVWR-02, DSHR-01, DSHR-02, DPRM-01; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac, with Portty installed and running, open Settings and check that the Desktop group shows no Background service row (the daemon is launchd-managed; Portty is listed in Login Items). Turn Always-available desktop on, use Set Up Permissions to grant Screen Recording and Accessibility, run Test Capture until it shows the thumbnail, and Copy Link.
  2. Send the link to the home Mac privately. Quit Portty from the menu bar so no Portty window or menu-bar item is left. On the home Mac open the link in Chrome, then in Safari, each with `#debug` added to the address.
  3. Leave both open for 2 minutes, then look at the office Mac's screen in each viewer at full size and at the browser's zoom of 100 %.
  PASS when: both browsers show the office Mac's screen with the Portty window closed; the debug read-out shows frames decoded rising, a frame width equal to the office display's native pixel width, `cspViolations` 0, and `Streaming to 2 viewers`; text on the office Mac's screen is sharp (no blur from scaling) on the Retina display; the first frame arrives within about 2 s on the direct path (settles VM row F2's 1.1 to 3.6 s over DERP; see M-157); the Settings step showed no Background service row.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the office Mac's real Retina display, its real tailnet direct path and a person at the Mac for the permission prompts; the VMs always connect via DERP and show only a 2560 x 1440 virtual display.
  - _Source:_ 19-VM-CHECKS.md rows M1, V7, L1, V4, F1, F2 and UI-SPEC "UI Considerations" (Retina sharpness).

- **M-174** On battery the desktop lets the display sleep without a viewer, holds it awake with one, and a joining viewer lights a sleeping display (DPRM-04; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac (or the home Mac as a stand-in if the office Mac is always on power) unplug power with Always-available desktop on and no viewer. Run `pmset -g assertions` and record it.
  2. Open the desktop link from the other Mac and record `pmset -g assertions` again; close the viewer, wait 15 s and record it a third time.
  3. With the viewer closed let the display sleep (`pmset displaysleepnow`), wait 20 s, then open the link from the other Mac. Plug power back in and record `pmset -g assertions` once more with no viewer.
  PASS when: on battery with no viewer there is no Portty "always-available desktop on AC power" assertion and the display sleeps by the Mac's own policy; with the viewer there are two Portty "desktop viewer connected" assertions (system sleep and display sleep) and they are gone 15 s after the viewer closes; joining while the display sleeps lights the display and the first frame arrives within a few seconds; on power with no viewer the "always-available desktop on AC power" system-sleep assertion is held.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real battery and power source; the VM has no battery, so row P1 shows only the on-power half.
  - _Source:_ 19-VM-CHECKS.md rows P1 and F7 (the VM halves), 19-09-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-175** An iPhone Home Screen link is named Portty Desktop, starts from a tap, and keeps working after a daemon restart and after an upgrade (DESK-03, DSHR-02, DVWR-02; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac turn Always-available desktop on and Copy Link. On the iPhone (Tailscale on) open the link in Safari, tap Share, Add to Home Screen, and read the suggested name before saving.
  2. Open the Home Screen icon. If the picture does not start, tap Tap to Start. Then on the office Mac choose Quit All and open Portty again, and open the icon again. Repeat after an upgrade of Portty on the office Mac.
  PASS when: the suggested and saved name is "Portty Desktop"; the icon opens the viewer without asking for a login and shows the office Mac's screen after at most one tap; the same icon works after the daemon restart and after the upgrade with no new link. Do not turn the desktop switch off during this item: that revokes every saved desktop link by design (the Settings footnote says so).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ a physical iPhone and iOS's Home Screen behaviour (name taken from the page, token kept on launch); the VM rows show the link text surviving both events but not the phone.
  - _Source:_ 19-VM-CHECKS.md rows F4 and F5 (link text unchanged), UI-SPEC "UI Considerations" (iPhone Home Screen name and relaunch).

- **M-176** The daemon delivers the desktop-approval lapse notification itself when the Portty window is closed, on a notarized build (DPRM-03; the owner runs it; human_needed, listed here so it is not folded into a pass).
  1. Install the notarized release on the office Mac, allow notifications for Portty when macOS asks, turn Always-available desktop on, and quit the Portty window so only the background service runs.
  2. Get a lapse. Either wait for the real monthly Screen Recording re-approval prompt (the same wait as M-160), or install a Developer ID build made with the `vmdesktop` tag (never published; the rig in 19-VM-CHECKS.md row F13 builds one), post the simulated lapse through its debug route, then reinstall the untagged build.
  3. Read the notification that appears, then open the Portty window and cause a second lapse the same way.
  PASS when: with the window closed one banner appears with the title "Portty" and the text "Portty desktop needs approval at the Mac."; with the window open only one banner appears, posted by the window; a second lapse replaces the first banner rather than stacking another. If no banner appears with the window closed, that is a defect to fix, not a gap to accept. The VM rows V1b and F13 passed on a Developer ID build after the first run (V1) failed and was fixed in 19-14, so what is open here is the banner itself and the notarized build, which a VM guest cannot show.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real on-screen banner under the daemon's bundle identity on a notarized build; the VM guest screen reads blank and the VM rows stand in with the notification database and the unified log.
  - _Source:_ 19-VM-CHECKS.md rows V1 (failed, superseded), V1b and F13; UI-SPEC "UI Considerations" (lapse notification posted by the daemon with the GUI closed).

- **M-177** An upgrade on the office Mac with a desktop viewer attached and live terminal sessions (DSHR-01, DSHR-02, DESK-03, DPRM-03; the owner runs it; human_needed; repeats VM row F5b on the real Mac and reads the tick gap).
  1. On the office Mac with Always-available desktop on, start two sessions (one that prints a counter every second after 300 history lines) and open both the desktop link and a terminal share link from the home Mac in Safari.
  2. Install the next version over the running one (M-169's update path, or the DMG replace) and watch both pages for 2 minutes; then open Settings on the office Mac.
  PASS when: both shells keep their pids and the counter shows no missing or repeated number except one pause of at most a few seconds at the moment the new daemon first starts (the VM showed 3 to 4 s with the cause unproven; record the length you see); the history is intact; the desktop viewer shows Reconnecting at most twice, about 35 s apart, and returns to streaming on the same link with no reload; Settings does not say "Paused after repeated errors" and the Desktop status reads Ready or Streaming; the Desktop group still shows no Background service row afterwards.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ the office Mac's real upgrade, its own tailnet path and the owner's sessions; the VM run (rows F5 and F5b) used a Developer ID build over DERP and could not name the cause of the pause.
  - _Source:_ 19-VM-CHECKS.md rows F5 and F5b and the "Findings from 19-15 (F5)" section.

- **M-178** The Safari consoles on the home Mac and on the iPhone show no Content Security Policy violation on the /desktop/ page (DVWR-02; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the home Mac, in Safari's Develop menu, turn on the Web Inspector for the desktop page, open the link and leave the Console tab open for 2 minutes while the picture streams.
  2. Repeat on the iPhone: connect it to the home Mac by cable (or Wi-Fi debugging), open the link in iPhone Safari, choose the iPhone in the Develop menu and read its Console. Do the same from the Home Screen icon of M-175 if the inspector offers it.
  PASS when: neither console shows a line starting "Refused to" (script, style, connect, media or manifest), and the page still streams.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ real Safari on macOS and iOS with Web Inspector; the VM rows V7 and M1 read `cspViolations` 0 in Safari and Chrome, and the Chrome end-to-end test `TestDesktopCSP_E2E` runs only with the `e2e` tag.
  - _Source:_ 19-VM-CHECKS.md rows M1 and V7; 19-06-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-179** How the Desktop group, the setup sheet, the tray line and the viewer look in real windows (DPRM-01, DPRM-02, DSHR-01; the owner runs it; human_needed; the VM's WebKit screenshots are blank, so no one has seen these).
  1. On the office Mac open Settings, Sharing, in light and then dark appearance. Look at the Desktop group (switch, Status, Link, Permissions, Background service when shown) against the other groups: spacing, alignment, dimming while Getting a link, and the macOS-native feel.
  2. At the dialog's normal 560 px height, find the Background service row (to see it, disable Portty's background item in System Settings, Login Items, and restart Portty) and scroll to its Open Login Items button; decide whether the scroll is acceptable.
  3. Turn off Screen Recording and Accessibility for Portty, open Set Up Permissions and read the how-to lines; run Test Capture with Screen Recording on and look at the 256 x 160 thumbnail box (a wide and a tall display if you have both). Re-enable both grants.
  4. With the desktop on, open the menu-bar menu with no viewer and with a viewer attached, then look at a viewer's chip and cards in the home Mac's browser and on the iPhone in light and dark.
  PASS when: the group looks like part of the Settings sheet in both appearances; the Background service button is reachable by scrolling and the owner accepts that (or asks for a taller dialog); the how-to names panes that exist in System Settings (Screen & System Audio Recording, Device Control and Data Access on macOS 27) and wraps in 4 lines or fewer; the thumbnail sits in its fixed box without cropping or moving the layout; the tray shows one dimmed, unclickable Desktop line ("Desktop: Ready", "Desktop: Streaming to 1 viewer"); the viewer's chip and cards read clearly over the black letterbox in light and dark.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run
  - _Why not automatable:_ visual judgement of real WebKit and AppKit rendering, which the VM cannot screenshot and the host may not run; structure is covered by `SettingsSheet.desktop.test.tsx`, `DesktopSetupSheet.test.tsx`, `DesktopView.test.tsx` and `TestDesktopTrayLine`.
  - _Source:_ 19-13-SUMMARY.md gap G8, 19-14-SUMMARY.md (tray line not observed), 19-VM-CHECKS.md findings (Background service row below the fold; setup sheet wording not exercised), UI-SPEC "UI Considerations" (appearance backstop).

### Category BK — Remote Control (Phase 20)

Rig: the user's office Mac and personal MacBook Air (the viewer) on the user's tailnet, the user's iPhone with Tailscale on, and an external display; Portty installed from a Developer ID build or the first v2.0 release candidate. Nothing here runs without the owner at the Mac, and no token, link or address is written down: links go from the office Mac to the other devices through a private channel and are deleted afterwards. The VM rows these items extend are in `.planning/phases/20-remote-control/20-VM-CHECKS.md` (each item names its rows as _Source_); only what a VM cannot show is here. Every item that needs Control starts with the viewer's Control segment on and, where a window or display is named, on the office Mac. Items that say "recorded" are observations to write down, not pass or fail, because the plan could not settle them in a VM. Phase 21 numbers its items after M-193.

- **M-180** Pointer accuracy on the real Retina display and an external display at any viewer window size, and scrolling with a real trackpad (DCTL-01, DCTL-02; Portty 20-06; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac share the main (Retina) display and open the viewer on the home Mac in Chrome and then in Safari. With a text editor open, click the first character, a middle character and the last character of a line, and the close button of a window, once with the viewer window about 600 px wide and once full screen.
  2. Share the external display (or the second display) and repeat the clicks. Also click in the black letterbox bars around the picture.
  3. Double-click a word and triple-click a line in the editor.
  4. Scroll a long document with a two-finger trackpad swipe on the home Mac, with Natural scrolling on and then off in the home Mac's Trackpad settings, and again with the office Mac's own setting flipped.
  PASS when: every click lands on the character or button under the viewer's pointer, with no offset and no drift near the edges, at both window sizes on both displays; clicks in the letterbox bars do nothing on the office Mac; double and triple click select the word and the line at the Mac's normal double-click pace; the document scrolls the same way the viewer's own page does for the same gesture, whichever way the office Mac's own scroll setting is (VM row S1 saw the setting not re-invert posted scrolls). Record any combination that differs.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real Retina and external displays and a physical trackpad; the VM has one virtual 2560 x 1440 display and posts scrolls from a script. Normalization and the rect math are unit-tested (`desktopInput.test.ts`, `TestInputMap_Point`).
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md rows E2 and S1, 20-UI-SPEC.md "UI Considerations" (pointer accuracy backstop), 20-06-PLAN.md.

- **M-181** Input methods, emoji picker, dictation and dead keys arrive once, and a non-US viewer layout is recorded (DCTL-02; Portty 20-06; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. In Chrome and then Safari on the home Mac, open the viewer with Control on and click into a text field on the office Mac.
  2. Turn on a Japanese or Chinese input method, compose a phrase, choose a candidate and commit it. Note where the candidate window appears.
  3. Open the emoji picker (Ctrl+Cmd+Space) and insert an emoji; start dictation and say a sentence; type Option+E then E for an accented letter.
  4. Switch the home Mac to a non-US layout (for example German or French) and type letters, punctuation and symbols.
  PASS when: the composed phrase, the emoji, the dictated sentence and the accented letter each arrive on the office Mac exactly once, with no raw letters ahead of a composition; the candidate window sits where the hidden input is, near the picture. Recorded for step 4: for each key that types a different character on the office Mac than on the viewer, the viewer layout, the key and both characters (printable keys go by physical key code, so this list is the input to a layout fallback decision).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real input methods, the emoji picker, macOS dictation and physical keyboard layouts in real browsers; jsdom sends synthetic events only.
  - _Source:_ 20-UI-SPEC.md "UI Considerations" (input-method and emoji backstop), 20-06-SUMMARY.md backstop truths, 20-02-SUMMARY.md (layout fallback observation), 20-06-PLAN.md.

- **M-182** Nothing stays held after a Cmd chord, a closed tab, a sleeping home Mac or a dropped network (DCTL-02; Portty 20-06; the owner runs it; human_needed; extends VM row H1).
  1. With Control on and a text editor focused on the office Mac, press Cmd+C and then type letters, in Chrome, Safari and Firefox.
  2. Hold Shift in the viewer and close the viewer tab with Shift still down; at the office Mac type a letter on its own keyboard.
  3. Hold a letter key in the viewer and close the home Mac's lid for 30 s; open it and look at the office Mac's editor.
  4. Hold a letter key in the viewer and turn the home Mac's Wi-Fi off for 15 s, then on.
  PASS when: after each case the editor shows no repeating key, a letter typed at the office Mac's own keyboard comes out in lowercase and Cmd is not held (typing letters types letters, not shortcuts), and the viewer after it reconnects works with a new first key.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a physical keyboard, a sleeping laptop and a real network drop; the VM row H1 closed one Safari session only.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md row H1, 20-06-SUMMARY.md backstop truths (Cmd chord in three browsers), 20-06-PLAN.md.

- **M-183** Send Keys (Cmd+Tab, Cmd+Space, Lock Screen) and the Ctrl and Cmd swap from a non-Mac keyboard (DCTL-03; Portty 20-06 and 20-07; the owner runs it; human_needed; extends VM rows C1 and C2).
  1. With Control on, open Send Keys and choose Cmd+Tab, then Cmd+Space, then press Esc.
  2. Choose Lock Screen…, read the dialog (Don't Lock has focus) and choose Lock Screen. With the Mac locked, type letters in the viewer. Unlock the office Mac at its keyboard.
  3. On a non-Mac keyboard (a PC keyboard on the home Mac) turn Swap Ctrl and Cmd on: copy and paste in an office app with Ctrl+C and Ctrl+V and switch apps with Ctrl+Tab. Turn it off and press Ctrl+C in a terminal.
  4. Press Ctrl+Option+Cmd+Return in the viewer.
  PASS when: Cmd+Tab switches the office Mac's app and Cmd+Space opens search with nothing left held; Lock Screen only locks after the confirm, the viewer shows Locked, nothing typed while locked reaches the login window's password field, and the viewer returns to streaming after the unlock; with Swap on, Ctrl+C and Ctrl+V act as Cmd+C and Cmd+V and Ctrl+Tab as Cmd+Tab, with Swap off Ctrl+C reaches the terminal as Ctrl+C; the release chord lets go of every key and puts focus on the Control segment, and no part of it reaches the Mac.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real lock screen with its secure password field, and a physical non-Mac keyboard; the VM guest screen reads blank and has no second keyboard.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md rows C1 and C2, 20-07-PLAN.md, 20-06-PLAN.md.

- **M-184** A window share on the office Mac: raise, confinement, a banner over the window, chords and a hung app (DCTL-05; Portty 20-04 and 20-07; the owner runs it; human_needed; extends VM rows W1 and W2).
  1. Open TextEdit and Notes on the office Mac with TextEdit behind Notes. In the viewer's picker choose the TextEdit window and click inside it; click inside it again.
  2. Minimize TextEdit and click inside the picture.
  3. Put something over the shared window (a notification banner, or the Fonts panel moved over it) and click on the viewer at the covered spot.
  4. From the viewer press Cmd+Tab, Cmd+Space and Ctrl+Cmd+Q while the window is shared.
  5. Share a display from a second viewer, then in Terminal on the office Mac run `kill -STOP` on the TextEdit process, click in the first viewer's picture, and move the pointer in the second viewer. Run `kill -CONT` afterwards.
  PASS when: the first click brings TextEdit forward and the second lands in it; the minimized window is restored by the click; the click on a covered spot does nothing in either window; the three chords do nothing to the office Mac while a window is shared; with TextEdit stopped the first viewer shows the raise-failed chip (its text names the window) and the second viewer's input is not delayed.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real window server with another app's banner, the corporate Mac's own window behaviour and a real hung app; the VM rows W1 and W2 used a probe route for the raise and covering.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md rows W1 and W2, 20-04-SUMMARY.md (raise ladder, hung-app timeout), 20-04-PLAN.md.

- **M-185** What a window share shows of a sheet, an Open and Save panel and a pop-up menu (DESK-05; Portty 20-04; recorded, not pass or fail; the owner runs it; human_needed; closes VM row W3).
  1. Share the TextEdit window. On the office Mac press Cmd+S so the Save sheet drops from the window; look at the viewer. Cancel it.
  2. Press Cmd+O for the Open panel; look at the viewer. Cancel it.
  3. Open a pop-up menu in the window (the Typeface pop-up in the Fonts panel, or the format pop-up in the Save sheet) and look at the viewer.
  Recorded: for each of the three, whether the viewer's picture shows it. If the picture lacks them (Apple's note for single-window capture is that it excludes such windows, 20-RESEARCH R10), say whether a window share is still usable for the owner's work or should offer a display share for those cases.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the decoded frame could not be read in either VM browser (WebKit's `drawImage` of the video throws in the guest); only a person looking at a real viewer settles it.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md row W3 and research assumption A13, 20-09-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-186** A pop-up menu of the shared app can be clicked in a window share, and another app's window still cannot (DCTL-05; Portty 20-04 post-VM fix X1, `TestWindow_SameAppPopupAllowed`; the owner runs it; human_needed; the VM recheck of X1 after the same-app carve-out).
  1. Share the TextEdit window. Open a pop-up menu of TextEdit that drops outside the window's frame or over it (the Typeface pop-up in the Fonts panel, or the format pop-up in the Save sheet) and, through the viewer, click one of its items.
  2. Move a Finder window over part of the shared window and click through the viewer at a spot the Finder window covers.
  PASS when: the menu item is chosen (the typeface or format changes and the menu closes); the click at the spot covered by the Finder window does nothing in either window. If the pop-up click is still dropped, that is a defect to fix (the VM saw it dropped before the fix), not a gap to accept.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real shared app's pop-up menus and another app's windows over it on the real window server; the VM recheck of the fix was not run.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md row X1 and its follow-up note, 20-04-SUMMARY.md "Post-VM fix (X1)".

- **M-187** The Accessibility focus read stays valid on the real Macs (DCTL-05; Portty 20-04; recorded; the owner runs it; human_needed; VM row X3).
  1. With a window share on and a click landing in the shared window, lock and unlock the office Mac, then click again in the picture.
  2. Change a scroll preference in System Settings (Natural scrolling), then click again. Quit and reopen the shared app, share its window again and click again.
  3. If a click is ever dropped (the picture does not react, the raise-failed chip shows), keep clicking every minute for up to 10 minutes and watch whether it recovers on its own.
  PASS when: after each event the next click inside the shared window lands within about 2 s. Recorded if not: the macOS version, the event that came before, and how long it took to recover. The VM saw the focus read fail with code -25204 for about ten minutes after a lock, a scroll toggle and a typing run, cause unknown, with the window gate failing closed (every press dropped).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ it depends on the real Macs' session history over minutes; it showed once in a VM and did not repeat.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md row X3 and research assumption A2, 20-04-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-188** Display switching with an external display, an unplugged display, a closed window, and the in-band resolution change on iPhone Safari and in Portty's own window (DESK-05, DESK-06; Portty 20-03 and 20-07; the owner runs it; human_needed; extends VM rows D1 and D2, the Wails and iPhone halves of A11).
  1. With an external display connected, open the viewer in Safari on the home Mac and open the picker. Switch Main Display, Display 2, a window, and back, each time reading the button (Switching…, then Showing …).
  2. Unplug the shared external display; read the viewer. Plug it back in.
  3. Share a window and close it; read the viewer. Share another window and minimize it; open the picker.
  4. Repeat the display to window switch on the iPhone in Safari and in the Portty window on the home Mac (a desktop peer tab).
  PASS when: every switch shows Switching… and then Showing …, the picture stays sharp and never goes black, the viewer never shows Reconnecting and never reloads, and the first frame of a still window arrives within about 1 s; unplugging the display and closing the window each show the fallback notice and the picture keeps playing; a minimized window leaves the picker but the share does not drop to the main display; the picture changes size in place on the iPhone and in the Portty window.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a physical display to unplug and a physical iPhone; the VM rows D1 and D2 covered Safari and Chrome only and its displays are virtual.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md rows D1 and D2 and research assumptions A11 and A12, 20-03-SUMMARY.md (switch and fallback rows), 20-07-PLAN.md.

- **M-189** Clipboard both ways in Chrome and Safari, a password manager item that never copies, and a 1 MB text (DCTL-06; Portty 20-05 and 20-08; the owner runs it; human_needed; extends VM rows P1 and P2).
  1. In Safari: copy text on the office Mac, choose Clipboard, Copy from Mac, then paste into a field on the home Mac. Copy different text on the home Mac and choose Paste to Mac; answer Safari's Paste prompt or use the Paste popover; press Cmd+V in an app on the office Mac.
  2. In Chrome: choose Paste to Mac for the first time and allow the clipboard permission; then reset the permission to Block and choose it again.
  3. Copy a password from a real password manager on the office Mac (its own copy action, which marks the item concealed) and choose Copy from Mac.
  4. Copy a text of exactly 1 MB (1048576 bytes) on each side and send it each way; then a text one byte longer.
  PASS when: in Safari the copy lands from the click itself or the Copy popover opens when Safari refuses and its text copies; Paste to Mac sets the office Mac's clipboard and nothing is typed into the focused app, and Cmd+V pastes the text; in Chrome the first Paste to Mac asks, Allow sends it and Block opens the Paste popover; the password manager item gives the hidden-item notice, nothing reaches the home Mac's clipboard and the password never appears on the page; the 1 MB text round trips both ways and one byte more is refused with the too-large notice.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real browsers' clipboard gesture and permission rules, Safari's Paste prompt and a real password manager; the VM rows P1 and P2 could not give Safari a real user activation and used a script for the concealed item.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md rows P1 and P2, 20-UI-SPEC.md "UI Considerations" (Safari clipboard backstop), 20-08-PLAN.md, 20-05-PLAN.md.

- **M-190** Paste from Other Apps on the corporate Mac: does macOS ask before the daemon reads the clipboard (DCTL-06; Portty 20-05 and 20-10; recorded; the owner runs it; human_needed; VM row P1, research assumption A10 and R14).
  1. On the corporate office Mac, with the Portty window closed and Always-available desktop on, copy text in another app. In the viewer choose Clipboard, Copy from Mac.
  2. Watch the office Mac's screen for any alert about pasting from other apps, and search System Settings, Privacy & Security for any paste setting for Portty.
  3. If an alert shows, leave it unanswered and click Copy from Mac again; then answer it and try again.
  PASS when: either no alert appears and the copy completes, or the alert path behaves as follows: with the alert unanswered the viewer shows "Waiting for approval at the Mac. macOS is asking before Portty reads its clipboard." within about 2 s and the desktop keeps streaming, and after the alert is answered the copy completes. Recorded: whether an alert showed, its wording, and the setting's path if there is one. An alert on this Mac means 20-10's `PASTE_ROW: none` decision was wrong for it: raise it, because the setup sheet then needs a Paste from Other Apps row.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the corporate Mac's managed privacy settings; the VM's daemon read and wrote the clipboard with `accessBehavior` set to always allow and never showed an alert.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md rows P1 and P3 and research assumption A10, 20-09-SUMMARY.md (decision `PASTE_ROW: none`), 20-10-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-191** Chrome Keyboard Lock, the full-screen notice in Safari and Firefox, and the control bar, menus, popovers and chips in light and dark (DCTL-02, DCTL-03, DCTL-06; Portty 20-07 and 20-08; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. In Chrome on the home Mac choose Full Screen from the bar, allow Keyboard Lock if asked, and press Cmd+Tab and Cmd+Space with Control on. Hold Esc for 2 s to leave.
  2. In full screen open the Send Keys menu, the Lock This Mac dialog, the picker and both Clipboard popovers.
  3. Repeat Full Screen in Safari and in Firefox and read the notice.
  4. In light and then dark appearance on the home Mac look at the bar, its menus, both popovers and the stacked state chips (a window share with the raise-failed chip, and the Control needs Accessibility chip).
  PASS when: in Chrome Cmd+Tab and Cmd+Space reach the office Mac in full screen (and not the home Mac) and a 2 s Esc hold leaves full screen and lets go of every key; the menus, dialog and popovers are visible inside full screen; Safari and Firefox show "Full screen is on. This browser keeps Cmd shortcuts; use Send Keys for Cmd+Tab and Cmd+Space."; every bar item, menu, popover and chip reads clearly and aligns in both appearances.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Chrome's real Keyboard Lock permission and Esc hold, each browser's full-screen rules and a visual judgement in both appearances; the VM's WebKit screenshots are blank.
  - _Source:_ 20-UI-SPEC.md "UI Considerations" (Chrome Keyboard Lock and light and dark backstops), 20-07-SUMMARY.md and 20-08-SUMMARY.md manual lists, 20-07-PLAN.md.

- **M-192** Typing into a password field and Caps Lock reach the office Mac (DCTL-02; Portty 20-02 and 20-06; recorded; the owner runs it; human_needed; research assumptions A5 and A8, not run in the VM).
  1. With Control on, focus a password field in an app on the office Mac that is not the lock screen (a login form in Safari, or an app's password sheet) and type a short password through the viewer.
  2. Turn Caps Lock on at the home Mac's keyboard and type letters into a text editor on the office Mac; turn it off and type again.
  PASS when: the password field fills with the characters typed (a field that stays empty is a defect: macOS may be dropping posted keys while Secure Event Input is on); with Caps Lock on the editor shows capitals and with it off lowercase. Recorded: the app and macOS version if either fails. Nothing is typed on the lock screen; that is M-183.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Secure Event Input and a real keyboard's Caps Lock state on the real Macs; the VM's lock gate stopped all input while locked, so A5 was never measured.
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md closure (research assumptions A5 and A8, not run), 20-02-PLAN.md.

- **M-193** A viewer that cannot get a picture says so, and never says Streaming ahead of one (DCTL-01, DESK-06; Portty 20-06; the owner runs it; human_needed; VM row X2).
  1. On the home Mac open the link in Chrome and then in Safari, each with `#debug` added to the address, and watch the status and the frames decoded from the first second.
  2. Open the link from a network that cannot carry the media path (a phone hotspot or a guest network that blocks peer traffic) and wait for the page to give up.
  PASS when: in either browser the status reads Streaming only once the media path is up, so frames decoded start rising within a couple of seconds of it and a stuck connection never reads Streaming (record the gap you see between Streaming and the first frame; the VM saw Safari at 0 frames on its second poll); on the blocked network the viewer shows "Can't reach this Mac" within about 30 s and backs off between tries instead of retrying at once.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the VM's media path always connects, so a real connection failure and the real direct path's timing are not reachable, and `connectionState` is not exposed by the debug hook (X2 inferred it).
  - _Source:_ 20-VM-CHECKS.md row X2, 20-06-SUMMARY.md manual list item (d), 20-06-PLAN.md.

### Category BL — Portty App and iPhone Viewers (Phase 21: DVWR-01, DVWR-03, DVWR-04)

Status: 12 items, 0 PASS, 0 FAIL, 12 DEFERRED (2026-11-27, deferred to user UAT, 21-UAT.md). DVWR-01: partial (deferred M-201, M-204, M-205). DVWR-03: partial (deferred M-194, M-195, M-196, M-197, M-198, M-199, M-200). DVWR-04: partial (deferred M-202, M-203, M-205). The VM closure lines `DVWR01_VM: pass` and `DVWR04_VM: pass` stand; no requirement is complete until its deferred items pass.

Rig: the user's iPhone (Safari, then the Home Screen icon) signed in to the same Tailscale account as the user's two MacBook Airs, the office MacBook Air with Always-available desktop on (Portty Settings, Sharing), and the home MacBook Air running the Portty app and viewing the office one in a tab. The build under test is installed on both Macs by the owner; nothing here is installed or changed on any other Mac. No token, link or address is written down: the iPhone gets the office Mac's always-available desktop link through a private channel, and the link is deleted afterwards. The VM rows these items extend are in `.planning/phases/21-portty-app-and-iphone-viewers/21-VM-CHECKS.md` (each item names its rows as _Source_); only what a VM cannot show is here: a real phone's touch screen and Safari, the two real Macs' keyboards, displays and power settings, and a visual judgement. The control-mode release chord is Ctrl+Option+Cmd+Shift+Return (Ctrl+Option+Cmd+Return is taken by the system, VM row K3). Items record a date, PASS, FAIL (with what was seen) or DEFERRED; the user's note never carries a hostname, address or link. Phase 22 numbers its items after M-205.

- **M-194** One finger moves the pointer like a trackpad; a tap clicks, a double-tap double-clicks and selects a word at the Mac's pace over the tailnet, and press-and-hold right-clicks with no iOS callout (DVWR-03; Portty 21-03 and 21-10; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the iPhone open the office Mac's desktop link in Safari and tap Tap to Start. Open a text editor on the office Mac with a paragraph of text.
  2. Drag one finger across the screen in short and long strokes.
  3. Tap on a button or text field; double-tap a word; double-tap a word a second time more slowly.
  4. Press and hold on a file or a text selection for a second.
  PASS when: the pointer follows the finger relatively (it does not jump to where the finger lands) and the Mac's cursor is shown where the pointer is; a tap clicks once; a quick double-tap selects the word and a slow one is two clicks; the hold opens the Mac's right-click menu with no iOS magnifier, text selection, share sheet or link preview on the phone. Record the double-tap gap that still selects.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-27, DEFERRED (deferred to user UAT, 21-UAT.md; the owner was not available and did not run it).
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real touch screen, Safari's callout and the real tailnet latency; the unit tests drive synthetic touch events (`touchTrackpad.test.ts`, `DesktopPhoneLayer.test.tsx`) and the VM has no touch input.
  - _Source:_ 21-UI-SPEC.md "UI Considerations" (iPhone gestures backstop), 21-03-SUMMARY.md and 21-10-SUMMARY.md, 21-13-PLAN.md.

- **M-195** Tap then drag moves a window by its title bar, and two fingers scroll a long document in the direction the fingers move (DVWR-03; Portty 21-03 and 21-10; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the iPhone, tap and then immediately drag a window by its title bar on the office Mac; lift the finger to drop it.
  2. Open a long document or web page on the office Mac. Drag two fingers up, then down.
  3. Flip Natural scrolling in the office Mac's Trackpad settings (then flip it back) and repeat step 2.
  PASS when: the window follows the finger and drops where it was released with no stuck button afterwards; with two fingers the content moves with the fingers (fingers up, content up) whatever the office Mac's own scroll setting is. Record any setting where it reverses.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-27, DEFERRED (deferred to user UAT, 21-UAT.md; the owner was not available and did not run it).
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real touch screen and the real direction convention; the direction rule is unit-tested (`desktopCoords.test.ts` `scrollDelta`) but cannot be judged by feel.
  - _Source:_ 21-UI-SPEC.md "UI Considerations" (two-finger scroll direction backstop), 21-03-SUMMARY.md, 21-10-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-196** Pinch zooms to 5x and back, the view follows the pointer at an edge, a two-finger double-tap and Fit to Screen reset, and Safari's tab overview never opens (DVWR-03; Portty 21-03 and 21-10; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the iPhone pinch out slowly until it stops; pinch in; pinch out again.
  2. While zoomed, move the pointer to each edge of the picture.
  3. Two-finger double-tap; zoom again and tap Fit to Screen.
  4. Pinch in hard with all five fingers and pinch out near the screen edges, in Safari and from the Home Screen icon.
  PASS when: the zoom stops at 5x (the Fit to Screen label shows the live zoom, for example 2.5x); the view pans to keep the pointer on screen at an edge; both resets return to the fitted picture; Safari's tab overview never opens and no edge swipe goes back during a pinch. Record any gesture that does.
  - _Last run:_ 2026-11-27, DEFERRED (deferred to user UAT, 21-UAT.md; the owner was not available and did not run it).
  - _Why not automatable:_ Safari's own gesture recognisers (tab overview, edge swipe) exist only on a real phone; the maths and the non-passive listeners are unit-tested (`desktopCoords.test.ts`, `touchTrackpad.test.ts`).
  - _Source:_ 21-UI-SPEC.md "UI Considerations" (pinch and tab overview backstop), 21-03-SUMMARY.md (T-21-09 residual), 21-10-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-197** The soft keyboard types, Backspace and Return work, an armed modifier sends a chord, and the form-assist bar gap is the accepted one (DVWR-03; Portty 21-10; the owner runs it; human_needed).
