Re-run in both Safari and Chrome for iOS, with Cmd then a (Cmd+A) and the debug file's re-check list.

- **M-202** Control mode on the real Macs: Cmd+W, Cmd+Q and Cmd+C act on the office Mac only, the release chord Ctrl+Option+Cmd+Shift+Return gives Portty its shortcuts back, and after it a plain click is a plain click (DVWR-04; Portty 21-09, 21-12; the owner runs it; human_needed).

### Category BM — Click to Share for Tailnet People (Phase 22: DSHR-03, DSHR-04, DSHR-05, DSHR-06, DSHR-07, DCTL-04)

Status: not yet run. The VM proof (22-12, `.planning/phases/22-click-to-share-for-tailnet-people/22-VM-CHECKS.md`) ran G-01..G-11 on two macOS 27.2 VMs: nine PASS, G-04 and G-11 PARTIAL. SC1 and AR-21-01 are UNVERIFIED (no second Tailscale account) and Appendix B is PARTIAL. SC1 has never passed. Every item below is owner-run.

Rig: the owner's office MacBook Air as the shared Mac (Portty GUI and daemon, Share Desktop on) and the home MacBook Air in a browser as a guest, both on the owner's tailnet; items marked with a second account also need a Tailscale user who is not the owner (a shared-in node or a second login) with a device of their own. The build under test is installed by the owner on the Macs involved; nothing is installed or changed on any other Mac. No token, link, tailnet name or address is written down: the guest link goes to the guest by a private channel and is deleted afterwards. The VM rows these items extend are named as _Source_; only what a VM cannot show is here: a second Tailscale account, a restrictive access rule, real hardware input, a real menu bar, a real terminal and a person's judgement.

- **M-206** SC1: a second Tailscale user opens the guest link, waits at Admit, watches View only after Admit, and gets the own-devices refusal on a terminal link from the same device (DSHR-03, DSHR-04; Portty 22-01, 22-04, 22-07; the owner runs it; human_needed; UNVERIFIED, never passed).
  1. On the office Mac start Share Desktop and send the guest link to a person signed in to a different Tailscale account (a node shared into the owner's tailnet, or a second login), who opens it in a browser on their own device.
  2. Watch the guest page and the office Mac: the page waits, the Admit sheet names the person with their login name. Press Admit. Open the page's width at about 375 pt (a phone, or a narrow window) and read the cards.
  3. From that same guest device try to open a shared terminal session's link, and try the owner's own-account desktop link.
  PASS when: before Admit the page shows the waiting card and no picture; after Admit it shows the picture with the View only chip and nothing the guest types or clicks reaches the office Mac; both terminal and own-account links answer the refusal page, not a session; the cards fit at 375 pt with nothing clipped. Record whether the Admit sheet showed the guest's real login name.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. UNVERIFIED in the VM: "skip SC1", no second Tailscale user on the test tailnet (22-VM-CHECKS.md row SC1). The guest flow between two nodes of the same account passed (G-01, G-02, G-04, G-07).
  - _Why not automatable:_ a second Tailscale account and the real WhoIs on a real selected ICE pair; every automated test fakes `LookupPeer` (`TestTailnetPeer_Admits`, `TestTailnetPeer_Refuses`, `TestGuestRoutes_OtherAccountRefusedElsewhere`).
  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row SC1, 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 1, 22-06-SUMMARY.md UAT candidates, 22-07-SUMMARY.md manual candidate.

- **M-207** AR-21-01: a node shared in from another account is not offered as a Desktop row or terminal peer, and a hand-typed connect is refused (DSHR-03; Portty 22-10; the owner runs it; human_needed; UNVERIFIED).
  1. On a real tailnet where one of the owner's Macs runs Portty, share another account's node into the tailnet (or join one) and bring it online.
  2. On the home Mac open the sidebar and Settings. Look for that node as a peer, as an Other Macs entry and as a Desktop row.
  3. In a terminal on the home Mac run `portty attach <that node's host>:<session>`, and try the same host name through the Desktop connect path if one is offered.
  PASS when: the shared-in node appears nowhere in the sidebar, and a hand-typed connect to it is refused as an unknown host with no request made to it. Record the refusal text.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. UNVERIFIED in the VM (22-VM-CHECKS.md row AR-21-01): it needs a node from another account.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a live tailnet with a shared-in node of another account; the filter is unit-tested (`TestDiscoverPeers_OwnAccountOnly`, `TestDiscoverPeers_FailsClosedWithoutSelfIdentity`, `TestPeerConnect_RefusesHostMissingFromDiscovery`, `TestPeerDesktop_RefusesHostMissingFromDiscovery`).
  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row AR-21-01, 22-10-SUMMARY.md, 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 2.

- **M-208** A restrictive tailnet access rule blocks the guest and the page says Can't reach this Mac with the access-rules hint (DSHR-03; Portty 22-07; the owner runs it; human_needed; NOT RUN).
  1. On a tailnet whose access rules the owner controls, deny the guest's device the shared Mac on tcp/7444 and udp/7444.
  2. Open the guest link from that device.
  3. Allow tcp/7444 and udp/7444 from the guest's device to the shared Mac, wait for the rule to apply, and reload the page.
  PASS when: with the rule in place the page shows Can't reach this Mac with the hint to ask the tailnet admin to allow tcp/7444 and udp/7444 (the README says the same), and Reload Page keeps showing it; after the rule is relaxed the page reaches Admit. Record which of the two ports was blocked when the picture failed but the page loaded (page works over tcp, media needs udp).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. NOT RUN in the VM: no restrictive policy is available on the test tailnet (22-VM-CHECKS.md, owner-run item 11).
  - _Why not automatable:_ it needs a tailnet policy edit and a second device; the card text is unit-tested (`DesktopView.guest.test.tsx` "Can't reach this Mac").
  - _Source:_ 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 11, 22-CONTEXT.md specifics (the docs mention ACLs), README.md.

- **M-209** The tray sharing icon is legible beside the other tray icons in a light and a dark menu bar (DSHR-06; Portty 22-08; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac with other menu-bar icons present, start a share with no guest and then with one guest connected, in a light menu bar and again in a dark one (System Settings, Appearance, and a dark and a light wallpaper behind the bar).
  2. Compare the sharing icon (a P with a filled dot) with the not-sharing P and with the error and progress icons.
  PASS when: the sharing icon is told apart from the normal one at a glance in both menu bars, the dot is visible and not clipped at 18 px, and it returns to the normal icon when the share stops. The tray menu reads Desktop shared with nobody yet, then Desktop shared with 1 person, with Stop Sharing directly under it. Record the menu-bar background where the dot was hardest to see.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. VM row G-08: PASS in a light menu bar only (22-VM-CHECKS.md); the dark variant was not looked at.
  - _Why not automatable:_ a visual judgement on a real menu bar over real wallpapers; the icon's bytes, size and ranking are unit-tested (`TestTrayIconSharing`, `TestTrayIconAsset`).
  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row G-08, 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 3, 22-08-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-210** The Admit sheet arriving while the owner types in a real terminal: no stray answer, and the guard feels right (DSHR-04; Portty 22-11; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac start a share and keep typing in a Portty terminal (or Terminal.app) at normal speed, including Return, Space and Esc.
  2. Have a guest open the link so the Admit sheet appears in the middle of your typing; keep typing for a second.
  3. Then press Return, Space and Esc once each with the sheet showing, and click Admit within about half a second of it appearing, then again after a second.
  PASS when: no typed key answers the sheet (it stays, nobody is admitted or denied); a click inside about 600 ms does nothing and a later click admits; the characters typed before the sheet appeared reach the terminal and the ones typed after do not land in a hidden input. Record whether the guard feels too long or too short.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. Deferred at the 22-12 checkpoint (the VM rows drive the sheet by Accessibility, not a typing person).
  - _Why not automatable:_ it depends on a person's key timing and focus handoff; the guard and the keys are unit-tested (`AdmitSheet.test.tsx`: stray Enter, Space and Escape answer nobody; a click inside the guard does nothing).
  - _Source:_ 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 4, 22-11-SUMMARY.md manual candidates.

- **M-211** A right-to-left or look-alike display name on the real Admit sheet and the Share Desktop list keeps the login name visible (DSHR-04; Portty 22-02, 22-10, 22-11; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. In a Tailscale account the owner controls, set the display name to a right-to-left string (Arabic or Hebrew letters) and then to a look-alike of the owner's own name (a Cyrillic letter in place of a Latin one).
  2. Open the guest link from that account's device; read the Admit sheet; admit; read the Share Desktop sheet's Watching list.
  PASS when: the bold name does not reorder the text around it, the login name is on its own line and fully visible on both the sheet and the list, a very long name wraps instead of hiding the login, and no control or zero-width character changes the layout. Record the name used.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. Deferred at the 22-12 checkpoint.
  - _Why not automatable:_ it needs a real account name and real glyph shaping; the sanitising and the layout rules are unit-tested (`TestNames_Sanitize`, `AdmitSheet.test.tsx` and `ViewerList.test.tsx` bidi cases).
  - _Source:_ 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 5, 22-10-SUMMARY.md, 22-11-SUMMARY.md manual candidates.

- **M-212** "Someone is using this Mac" appears for a controlling guest while the owner types with real hardware (DCTL-04; Portty 22-03, 22-05, 22-07; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac admit a guest and Allow control. Have the guest type steadily in a text editor on the office Mac.
  2. Type on the office Mac's real keyboard, then move the real mouse and click, while the guest keeps typing.
  3. Stop touching the Mac and wait 3 seconds.
  PASS when: while the owner types or clicks, the guest's keys do not land and the guest page shows Someone is using this Mac for at least 2 seconds; nothing the guest typed is mixed into the owner's text; after about 3 seconds idle the guest's typing posts again. The chip never names the owner.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. Deferred at the 22-12 checkpoint; G-05 passed in the VM with a software stand-in (an untagged CGEvent click), which is not real hardware.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real keyboard and mouse hardware at the HID level; the arbiter and the tap are unit-tested with fakes (`TestArbiter_PhysicalWins`, `TestPhysical_UntaggedInputDropsAndReleases`) and Appendix B showed untagged hardware events reach a listen-only tap.
  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row G-05, 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 6, RESEARCH Appendix B.

- **M-213** Warp check and the Secure Keyboard Entry case on real hardware (DCTL-04; Portty 22-03, 22-11; the owner runs it; human_needed; completes RESEARCH Appendix B, recorded PARTIAL in `.planning/phases/22-click-to-share-for-tailnet-people/evidence/appendix-b-real-mac.txt`).
  1. Warp: with a guest controlling, make the guest move the pointer across the picture, then at once move the real mouse a short way on the office Mac.
  2. Secure Keyboard Entry: in Terminal.app turn on Secure Keyboard Entry (Terminal, Secure Keyboard Entry) or focus a password field, with a guest controlling, and type on the real keyboard for a few seconds; then have the guest type.
  3. Turn Secure Keyboard Entry off.
  PASS when: the owner's real mouse move registers right after a remote pointer warp (the warp does not swallow local input); while Secure Keyboard Entry is on, Portty shows the banner starting "Typing at this Mac isn't detected right now", the guest's keys still post, and the banner clears within a few seconds of turning it off. Record the macOS version and whether the owner's own typing is detected while the banner shows (the documented blind spot: it is not).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. Appendix B PARTIAL: it covered untagged hardware keys and mouse at a listen-only tap with no Input Monitoring prompt; the warp check and the real-hardware Secure Keyboard Entry case were not covered. VM row G-06 passed with the Terminal toggle in a VM (banner 1.38 s, gone 1.56 s).

- **M-214** Phase 20 M-05 with a real non-activating panel: guest keys are dropped as `window_key_focus` and nothing types into the panel (DCTL-05, DSHR-04; Portty 22-05; the owner runs it; human_needed; G-11 PARTIAL).

Record the launcher used.

  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row G-11, 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 8, 22-05-SUMMARY.md, Phase 20 M-05.

- **M-215** Stop Sharing with two real guests ends both quickly on a quiet machine (DSHR-05; Portty 22-05, 22-06; the owner runs it; human_needed; G-04 PARTIAL).
  1. On the office Mac start a share and admit two guests on two different devices, both streaming.
  2. Press Stop Sharing in the tray. Note when each guest page shows Sharing has ended (or time the leaves in the audit file).
  3. Repeat twice, then open the old link.
  PASS when: both guests leave within 2 seconds of one another and of the stop, on every repeat, with the machine otherwise idle; the old link answers This link no longer works. Record the spread each time and the machine's load.

  - _Why not automatable:_ two real devices and a quiet machine; the stop is unit-tested (`TestGuestStop_ThreeSilentPeersDoNotHoldItUp`, `TestDesktopShareAPI_StopCutsGuests`).
  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row G-04, 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 9.

- **M-216** The Admit sheet sits above the Share Desktop and Settings sheets, and the Portty window comes forward on a knock (DSHR-04, DSHR-06; Portty 22-09, 22-11; the owner runs it; human_needed).
  1. On the office Mac open Settings, then the Share Desktop sheet over it. Have a guest open the link.
  2. Hide Portty (Cmd+H) or put another app in front, and have a second guest knock.
  3. Click the notification banner for the knock; then with Portty frontmost have a third guest knock.

Record any case where the sheet appeared behind another.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. Not driven in the VM (22-12 notes): G-07 showed the sheet in the AX tree on a knock but not the stacking.
  - _Why not automatable:_ real window stacking, activation and the Notification Center banner click; the routing is unit-tested (`TestShareNotification_AdmitBringsWindowForwardAndNotifiesOnlyWhenHidden`, `TestShareNotification_ClickRouting`).

Record any event with no banner.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. VM row G-07 PASS for delivery: all three notifications were recorded in the notification database while the display was captured, but banners are transient and were not caught on screen (22-VM-CHECKS.md).
  - _Why not automatable:_ whether macOS shows a banner over a captured display is visual; delivery and the sentences are unit-tested (`TestShareNotification_JoinAndLeaveSentences`, `TestShareNotification_CoalescesWithinFiveSeconds`, `TestShareNotification_NameIsCappedAtEightyWithEllipsis`).
  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row G-07, 22-UI-SPEC.md ("a captured display may silence banners" row), 22-09-SUMMARY.md.

- **M-218** The Share stopped banner appears after the daemon dies mid-share, and an upgrade with a share running ends the share and says so (DSHR-05, DSHR-06; Portty 22-06, 22-11; the owner runs it; human_needed).

  1. On the office Mac start a share with a guest watching and choose Quit Portty, then Quit GUI Only; read the confirm sentence first.
  2. Start a share again and end the Portty GUI process abruptly (Force Quit) with a guest watching.
  3. Open Portty again (if it does nothing, use `open -n /Applications/Portty.app`).
  PASS when: the Quit confirm says quitting ends the share and all three choices remain; after Quit GUI Only the guest page shows Sharing has ended before the window hides and the tray indicator clears; a Force Quit ends the share within about a second; the next launch shows no stale tray state or banner. Record whether a plain double-click launch worked after the crash (VM row G-09 saw `open` answer -600 while the daemon survived).
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. VM row G-09 PASS for the GUI process ending (share ended in 0.51 s, `gui_gone`); `QuitGUIOnly` itself needs a Wails context and was not driven.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the real AppKit quit path and LaunchServices; the quit logic is unit-tested (`TestDesktopShareQuit_StopsAnActiveShareFirst`, `TestDesktopShareQuit_NoShareNoStop`, `TestDesktopShareQuit_SlowStopDoesNotBlockHiding`, `QuitConfirmModal.test.tsx` "share warning").
  - _Source:_ 22-VM-CHECKS.md row G-09, 22-09-SUMMARY.md deviation 3, 22-12-SUMMARY.md owner-run item 10.

Record anything read twice or not at all.

- **M-221** A browser viewer keeps its picture through a desktop source restart (stall recovery, wake) with no freeze (DPRM-03, DPRM-04; debug rtp-seq-restart-freeze; VM or real Mac; NOT RUN).
  1. On the office VM or a real Mac, open Desktop from a browser viewer (Chrome and Safari) and from the Portty app; keep the picture moving.
  2. Force a restart: sleep and wake the display, and separately let the watchdog restart a frozen stream (still screen with the pointer nudge blocked, or the vmdesktop rig's stall); note the "stall recovered" or wake line in daemon.log.
  3. Watch each viewer for 60 s after the restart; in Chrome, chrome://webrtc-internals for the inbound video and audio: packetsReceived keeps rising, packetsLost does not jump, framesDecoded resumes.
  PASS when: every viewer shows a moving picture within 2 s of the restart line and audio resumes; FAIL on a picture frozen for more than 2 s while daemon.log shows the stream running.
  - _Last run:_ not yet run. The fix is proven on the host with a pion loopback viewer only (`TestFanout_SourceRestartKeepsRTPSequence`).
  - _Why not automatable:_ a real browser's SRTP and jitter buffer and a real capture restart; pion's SRTP stands in for them in the Go test.
  - _Source:_ .planning/debug/rtp-seq-restart-freeze.md, Phase 23 deferred-items.md (23-02).

---

## 6. Standing Convention

**Every future phase that adds, renames, or removes tests must:**

1. **Automated tests** — add the new test files to the appropriate suite group (Go, vitest, Playwright, or build-script). No build tags, no file moves — the group delineation lives in this manifest only.

2. **Manual checklist** — if the phase introduces a behavior that cannot be automated (native GUI interaction, remote peer, live PTY, physical hardware), add a new M-NN item to Section 5 of this file with: behavior description, why it cannot be automated, and source phase/UAT log.

3. **Traceability map** — add a row to Section 4 for every new test file that covers a v4.0 (or later milestone) requirement. The path column must be a repo-relative file path ending in `.go`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, or `.sh` — no test names, no describe-block suffixes in the path column. Test names go in the Notes column. Run `bash tests/check-traceability-paths.sh` locally before committing to confirm the script exits 0.

4. **Branch protection maintenance** — if `build.yml` matrix entries change (platform added, OS label changed), re-apply branch protection with the updated check context names (see Section 3).

This convention is also referenced in the repo-level `CLAUDE.md` so it surfaces to every Claude session.
